- **Wallpaper-protection technique while scoring trim in place**: slide a plain plastic backing strip underneath the corner trim before scoring it with a hacksaw, specifically to avoid damaging adjacent wallpaper — cut ~50cm working sections at a time. [source: yt_nb3L-k69yx8]
- **Finish step**: seal all fitted trim edges/joints with sealant before handover. [source: yt_nb3L-k69yx8]

### Windows / Slopes — Material Selection: Why Never Plaster (added 2026-08-17)

> Folded into [[13_Surfaces_and_Finishes/analysis/Windows_Slope_Finishing|Windows: Slope Finishing]] (2026-08-18) — this section kept as the original store record.

`yt_AGjYrwqilNA` — direct explainer complementing `yt_nb3L-k69yx8`'s install-technique content with the material-selection reasoning. [source: yt_AGjYrwqilNA]

- **Plastered window slopes are never appropriate for a PVC window, for four mechanism-based reasons**: (1) PVC and plaster have mismatched thermal-expansion coefficients and no sealant bonds well to both — a frame/plaster crack reliably forms; (2) plaster itself cracks over time regardless of skill (no installer can guarantee crack-free past ~5 years vs. the window's 10-20 year rating) and painted plaster yellows within 1-4 years; (3) a plastered slope has no closing/retaining contour, so wallpaper's cut edge is always exposed and eventually peels; (4) plastering forces the frame's first foam joint to be trimmed flush/recessed — exactly the wrong compromise on this store's known weakest installation joint, which should always be maximized, never thinned.
- **Two legitimate exceptions where a thin decorative (uninsulated) slope is the *correct* choice, not a cost-cutting compromise**: a temporary/non-structural partition wall (no thermal/acoustic slope performance needed for a light stud-and-drywall divider); and a balcony/loggia glazing block's exterior-facing slope specifically, where the frame's mounting "quarter" ledge typically leaves only ~2-3cm clearance — too little to tuck an insulated sandwich panel behind the frame at that junction.
- **Five-point checklist for a correct insulated (sandwich-panel) slope**: mechanically fixed (not just glued) to carry a thick foam joint; true foam-core sandwich construction, never hollow-core (visible rib lines, worse insulation); a closing/retaining perimeter contour, not just butted-and-puttied; that contour must be a **snap-together two-piece system (inner J-profile + outer profile), never glued** (glued trim eventually detaches from sash-impact, snap-fit flexes and returns); both panel and profile must be **primary (virgin) plastic, never secondary/recycled** (unknown composition, visibly yellows within a few years — e.g. sill end-caps).
- **Skipping the closing contour entirely (butting raw hand-cut panel edge to wall, puttying, wallpapering over) is a named common installer shortcut with three concrete failure mechanisms**: hand-cut sandwich-panel edges are visibly wavy once puttied/papered; putty/wallpaper bonds far better to wall than to a panel's raw cut edge, so the joint reliably peels; sandwich panel is far softer than plaster, so a heavy sash's repeated impact flexes it enough to crack the puttied seam over time.
- **Buying caution**: many companies install only the window, declining slope work — a client left to DIY or hire a second firm risks the original installer later denying warranty claims by blaming third-party slope work. Recommend buying window + slope finishing as one bundled product with one combined warranty.
- **Installation sequence rule: skirting board always before the window/slope closing contour trim strip, never the reverse** — installing the trim strip first always leaves the skirting board pushed slightly proud of the wall when fitted up against it afterward, however thin the trim. `confirmed`, simple sequencing rule. [source: yt_fHdiWCw-EBQ]
- **DIY sash self-adjustment (before calling a professional)**: a 4mm hex key handles two adjustments — a bottom-hinge screw for vertical sash position (clockwise raises a sagging/catching sash), and an eccentric bolt around the sash perimeter for seal pressure (rotate the thicker lobe toward the seal to increase pressure). **Paper-test check**: close the sash on a sheet of paper at several points around the perimeter — pressure is too weak if the paper slides out easily, good if it resists or tears. Any other hardware adjustment is explicitly left to a professional. [source: yt_qwIRfgn1Tog]

- **Turn-only vs. tilt-and-turn sash hardware: tilt-and-turn is unambiguously the better default**, cost delta only ~1-2% of window price. Three advantages: avoids needing a hard fall-limiter/child-safety stop (turn-only swings fully open with no other restraint); gives trickle/slot ventilation without fully opening; and — the standout mechanism — **a large/heavy sash (e.g. ~2m×2m, which can't be split into a narrower pair without violating facade design) deforms within about a week of regular full-swing-open use**, since its own weight bears on the side hinges every opening; restricting such a sash to tilt-only operation transfers its weight to the bottom frame/transom instead, avoiding the deformation mechanism. `single-account`, genuinely new hardware-selection rule with an explained mechanism. [source: yt_XFrmhhM1ogg]

### Doors / Trim (Windows) — DIY PVC Window Installation Masterclass (fourth playlist batch, added 2026-08-17)

> Folded into [[13_Surfaces_and_Finishes/analysis/Windows_Installation_and_Fastening|Windows: Installation & Fastening]] and [[13_Surfaces_and_Finishes/analysis/Windows_Opening_Reconfiguration|Windows: Opening Reconfiguration]] (2026-08-18) — this section kept as the original store record.

`yt_6_cH35u4ouM` — single-speaker (Zemskov himself) consolidated DIY
masterclass covering frame fastening, foam-joint technique, hardware
quality, sill quality, and a worked opening-widening procedure. No
pricing. [source: yt_6_cH35u4ouM]

- **Window-opening "quarter" (reveal) thermal rationale**: a recessed
  quarter protects the first foam joint on all sides vs. a flush opening,
  improving thermal performance — but gives **no fastening advantage**
  (same wind load on the fasteners either way). `confirmed`.
- **Frame fastening must be direct (anchor or dowel through the profile),
  never via mounting plates alone** — plates are legitimate only for
  preliminary/temporary positioning, not final structural fastening.
  Frame drain holes near fastening points are a deliberate manufacturer
  feature (condensate prevention), not a defect.
- **Mounting wedges only, never wood scraps, for leveling/load-bearing
  support** — one exception: foam-joint gaps **>80mm** may use treated
  hardwood timber (never scrap softwood), always alongside proper wedges,
  never as their substitute.
- **Two-stage foam technique for a door/window sill joining seam**: apply
  foam, wait for primary cure (springy, non-tacky skin) before assembling
  — disturbing uncured foam collapses expansion and weakens the joint;
  apply a second smaller bead on top of the near-cured first bead to
  re-bond for a stronger final joint.
- **Three-stage perimeter foam-seam technique**: pre-fill large voids
  first; form the continuous seam at only **~25% fill initially** so it
  expands/cures properly; trim flush once cured. A seam formed in one pass
  at full thickness reliably develops void defects ("свищи") — even a
  3-4mm void (screw-diameter) defeats the window's thermal benefit
  entirely; failure (visible ice on the upper slope) typically appears
  within 2-3 years of a rushed one-pass seam. `confirmed`, mechanism +
  timeline.
- **4-point hardware (ленточная фурнитура) quality checklist**: (1) ≥4
  operating positions including trickle/slot ventilation (locked-closed
  but perimeter-circulating — demonstrated with a banknote test); (2)
  robust individual mechanism components (tilt-scissor hinge cited as a
  quality marker); (3) low-maintenance (periodic oil-port lubrication
  only); (4) foolproofing (can't combine open+tilt simultaneously); (5)
  anti-burglary resistance visible even in base configuration.
- **Water-sill (отлив) quality checklist**: factory-manufactured, not
  hand-bent (hand-bent tolerance 2-3mm vs. factory <1mm edge-to-edge);
  factory protective film **must be removed immediately after install**
  (leaving it on is a documented bad habit even among professional
  installers — the film partially bonds/partially peels within a few
  years and becomes unremovable); factory-applied three-layer
  sound/vibration-damping backing pad (adhesive + damping + insulation
  layers) — a hand-applied pad tends to delay and peel.
- **Balcony-block opening-widening procedure (worked example)**: reduce an
  oversized standard door (leaf ≈85cm from 90cm frame) to a **60-65cm
  optimal width** by moving the frame over with a lumber-framed
  extension — reclaims wall depth on the other side (worked example: ~50cm
  reclaimed to ~65cm, enough for a 65cm-deep refrigerator). Frame recesses
  ~3cm into the new quarter. Antiseptic applied by sprayer (same product
  volume as brushing, faster, less mess), ~24h cure before workable.
  Framing foam driven **≥3cm deep** into structural gaps; small gaps
  between lumber pieces sealed with wrapped insulation first, foam second.
  **Beginner-mistake avoidance rule**: set the thinner outer lumber piece
  back ~7cm from the frame edge (not flush) to avoid having to notch a
  corner later for slope-panel fit. Foam cure before trimming: ~47
  minutes. **Fastening plates are the correct method specifically at a
  widened opening's header** when the header material (e.g. foam-block
  infill) can't hold direct fasteners — level/fasten one side fully
  before fastening the second side, or risk a permanently bowed frame.
- **On-site leveling/torque QC technique**: temporary support block used
  so a dowel/anchor can be fully torqued without bending the frame;
  correct torque continues **past** first visible plastic deformation at
  the fastener head, seating against internal reinforcement, then backs
  off half a turn — stopping at first deformation (a common mistake)
  under-tightens and holds nothing.

### Doors / Trim (Windows) — Fastening in Soft (Aerated-Concrete/Foam-Block) Walls (added 2026-08-17)

> Folded into [[13_Surfaces_and_Finishes/analysis/Windows_Installation_and_Fastening|Windows: Installation & Fastening]] (2026-08-18) — this section kept as the original store record.

`yt_gjTGr8j6-DA` — direct companion/edge-case to `yt_6_cH35u4ouM` (#007):
that source establishes direct anchor/dowel fastening as the default;
this source demonstrates the narrow legitimate exception when the base
material is too soft to hold a direct fastener. [source: yt_gjTGr8j6-DA]

- **When to use distributed mounting plates instead of direct fastening**:
  aerated-concrete/gas-block and foam-block walls are too soft for a
  direct anchor/dowel to hold under load (demonstrated: a drill bit sinks
  into the material with no hammer action at all; a fastener spins freely
  rather than gripping). Here, multi-point mounting plates are the
  *correct permanent* fastening method — the inverse of the #007 default,
  where plates are only for preliminary positioning.
- **Numeric fastening spec for soft-material walls**: dowel pre-drilled
  with an **8mm bit** for sections fastened to wood-based structural
  elements; mounting plates flush against the profile's structural
  shoulder, fastened into foam block with **4-7 self-tapping screws per
  plate**, screw length **90mm, no dowel/plug**; **plate spacing ≤60cm**
  (a 1.5m frame length typically uses 3 plates). Stated rationale: 5×
  4mm-diameter screws hold and resist impact better than 1× 8mm dowel in
  soft material, via load distribution and more points to defeat.
- **Hardened-screw caveat, soft material only**: hardened screws are
  normally avoided for window fastening (brittle in hard materials), but
  soft material cushions the impact load that would otherwise shatter a
  hardened screw — `single-account`, informally demonstrated (hammer
  strike on camera), not independently lab-verified.

### Plumbing — Convector-to-Bimetal Radiator Replacement Masterclass (new topic area, added 2026-08-17)

`yt_7MqiSqkO_cE` — dunk-format title ("ALWAYS CHANGE THIS CRAP!") hiding a
genuine detailed technique explainer — same misleading-title pattern this
store has already flagged for "how not to" titles, now confirmed to
extend to dunk-format titles too. No pricing. [source: yt_7MqiSqkO_cE]

- **Keep-vs-replace decision criteria**: keep an existing convector if
  undamaged, heats well, and has its own dedicated shutoff + regulator
  valve; replace if bulky/space-consuming and **not
  adjustable/regulated** — functional regulation capability is the
  deciding factor, not cosmetic condition.
- **Safety-critical warning**: verify the correct riser is shut off
  before cutting a radiator free — a basement worker can close the wrong
  riser by mistake. Cutting a still-pressurized pipe with a corded
  (mains-powered) grinder creates a near-guaranteed electrocution risk if
  water contacts the tool; if a convector has no dedicated shutoff and
  confirmation is uncertain, pierce a test hole with a torch first rather
  than grinding blind. `confirmed`, safety-critical, from direct
  practitioner experience.
- **Never mix heating-system pipe materials** — match the replacement
  radiator's pipe material to the existing riser system
  (polypropylene-to-polypropylene, metal-to-metal). Every
  material-transition joint is threaded and structurally weaker/leak-
  prone vs. a continuous or welded run; metal-to-metal welded runs also
  avoid the internal-diameter narrowing a threaded transition introduces.
- **False-wall pipe/riser concealment is the "good renovation" standard,
  with a quantified ~15cm windowsill-depth cost** — the belief that
  concealment "wastes floor area" is a myth (space above ~1m near a
  window is never functionally used); a wider windowsill is worth more
  than the depth reclaimed under it, and concealment avoids risky
  in-wall-only joints and clamp-related corrosion (see below). Leaving
  pipes fully exposed (boxed only at ceilings) is explicitly rejected as
  unacceptable for a quality renovation.
- **Bypass/jumper (байпас) is mandatory** whenever a radiator has its own
  shutoff valves — omitting it stops heating-medium circulation in that
  branch entirely when the valve is closed, cooling the floor above/below.
- **Pipe bending must be cold, with a proper pipe-bender tool, never
  heat-bent by hand** — heat-bending flattens the pipe's cross-section
  (demonstrated side-by-side), reducing flow; decline an installer who
  works without a proper bending tool.
- **Threaded-joint sealing**: flax/tow ("лён") + joint compound — the
  only acceptable sealing method per this source at time of filming.
- **Riser branch-point rule (the "50cm rule")**: the transition from
  standard riser spacing to the radiator's wider connection spacing must
  happen immediately at the riser, not partway along the run — branching
  mid-run creates unintended low points where air collects, causing
  intermittent airlock and the radiator periodically failing to heat.
  Related **pipe-slope rule**: pipes should rise slightly from radiator
  outward (return trending down, supply trending up) so air travels
  through the radiator rather than pooling.
- **3-step pipe corrosion-protection sequence, order matters**: (1) sand
  every joint/pipe until a light sheen appears; (2) degrease thoroughly
  (saturate, don't just wipe); (3) paint by brush only, generously, never
  spray (aerosol doesn't function as rust converter and can't coat the
  wall-facing side). **Explicit warning against "paint directly onto
  rust/dirt" product claims** — painting over unprepped rust/dirt traps
  moisture and accelerates corrosion underneath.
- **No reinforcing clamps/straps needed on concealed metal pipe runs** —
  metal pipe is strong enough unsupported behind a false wall; a
  clamped/strapped section is explicitly identified as the most likely
  point for that pipe to corrode and leak first.

### Walls / Ceilings — Drywall Partition Framing/Marking Techniques (partial extraction, added 2026-08-17)

`yt_oBP1cYCLARA` — commercial warehouse showcase/brag video ("1,000 SQ.M.
OF DRYWALL IN 1 DAY"), off-scope in scale/setting for this project; most
narrative content not extracted, only a few transferable techniques
below. [source: yt_oBP1cYCLARA]

- **Double-track vertical profile choice for a partition under impact
  load**: a single profile is "weak as a noodle" for a tall partition
  exposed to impact on both sides; a fully doubled/back-to-back profile
  setup is slower and more expensive with no meaningful rigidity gain
  over a simpler double-track approach for this use case. `single-account`
  tradeoff reasoning, not universally validated.
- **Template-based marking for large batches of identical framing cuts**:
  pre-mark one reference profile precisely once, then butt a stack of
  profiles against a stop/wall and transfer that single mark across all
  of them at once before cutting, rather than measuring each piece
  individually — a reusable time-saver for any project needing many
  identical framing cuts.
- **Chalk-line point-transfer marking for repeated spacing over a long
  run**: mark reference points with a tape measure at coarse intervals,
  then transfer/snap those points across intervening studs with a chalk
  line, rather than measuring and leveling each position individually.

### Mistakes / Warnings — PPE Necessity, First-Person Injury Account (added 2026-08-17)

- **Eye and hand protection are not optional even for an experienced
  installer** — the speaker recounts his own glove tearing and a cut
  from handling sharp cut metal-profile edges/swarf, and repeated metal
  shavings striking his safety glasses, during a large drywall-framing
  project. `confirmed`, first-person account of a real injury. [source:
  yt_oBP1cYCLARA]

### Electrical / Flooring — Completed-Project Tour, Partial Extraction (added 2026-08-17)

`yt_I3vcE3IRi0k` — heavily self-promotional company-showcase/handover
video with a direct sales pitch (contact the speaker for a design
project, then request his named employee as installer); most tour
content not extracted. [source: yt_I3vcE3IRi0k]

- **Electrical panel breaker labeling with photos**: each breaker
  individually labeled (with photos, not just text) so a client who's
  often not on-site can quickly identify which breaker controls which
  circuit without testing each one. `single-account`, genuinely reusable
  handover-quality practice.
- **Underfloor-heating thermostat pre-warm scheduling example**: schedule
  heating to start **15 minutes before** stated wake time so the floor is
  warm on rising while saving energy overnight (worked example: 7:00am
  wake → 06:45 start). `single-account`, timing specific to this
  floor/tile buildup, not a universal figure.
- **Floor-material transition seam should land exactly under the closed
  door**, so each room shows only its own flooring from its own side with
  the door shut. `confirmed`, simple placement rule, distinct from this
  store's existing tub/tile-junction rule.
- **Flood-resilience anecdote (single-account, promotional-adjacent)**: a
  neighbor's leak flooded this project's ceiling the night before
  handover; the ceiling itself was cheap/quick to replace, and the
  speaker credits moisture-resistant wallpaper, quartz-vinyl flooring,
  and enamel wall paint for walls/flooring/furniture surviving undamaged
  — plausible mechanism (moisture-resistant finishes limit water-incident
  damage), but framed self-promotionally; specific brand not adopted as
  neutral recommendation.

### Planning Rules / Design Process — Real Krasnodar Case Walkthrough (added 2026-08-17)

Team studio walkthrough of an actual ~110 m² Krasnodar project's real client
brief and 3D model, not a critique-format video. No pricing content.
[source: yt_gqc3jHl6YcM]

- **Stated design-process order**: (1) on-site survey — a 3D model built
  to ~1cm accuracy plus a written client brief/questionnaire, captured at
  the same visit; (2) concept development and client approval; (3) full
  3D model development and approval; (4) schematics/drawings — explicitly
  deprioritized as "tenth place" relative to getting the 3D model right
  first.
- **Client-brief checklist** (demonstrated on a real brief): intended
  ownership horizon/resale plans, family-planning status, pet ownership
  (directly drove a flooring decision — see Flooring below), both
  occupants' height (ergonomic/reach planning), sleep-schedule
  compatibility, allergies, vision, handedness. Reusable as a self-
  managed-owner planning checklist independent of whether a paid designer
  is used.
- **"Project within a project" — pre-wiring a room for a known future
  use-case change without redoing electrical later**: for a room expected
  to convert use (here: office/home-gym now → children's room later), both
  layouts' electrical points are installed during the single rough-in
  stage; the future layout's outlets are wallpapered over/hidden until the
  room's actual furniture layout changes. Two separate pull-up-bar wall
  reinforcements were pre-installed the same way (current wall + eventual
  future wall). `confirmed`, a genuinely reusable technique for any room
  expected to change use within one electrical rough-in lifecycle.
- **Match new-partition wall thickness across two openings purely for
  door-casing visual consistency**, even when their structurally-required
  thicknesses differ (145cm-clearance-driven wall vs. 325cm-zone-driven
  wall in this project) — avoids two doors in the same sightline having
  visibly different casing-reveal depths.
- **Toilet clearance ~80cm** — a second same-figure corroboration of this
  store's existing WC-clearance data from a different project.
- **Pet-driven flooring-material decision**: client's original preference
  (cork throughout) was overridden specifically because cork wears
  quickly under cat claws (2 cats). Resulting split: engineered wood board
  throughout except wet rooms, quartz vinyl (LVT-type) in kitchen and
  entry hall specifically (explicitly stated as inappropriate rooms for
  engineered board). Client separately rejected linoleum as looking
  "cheap" despite the designer's view that this isn't necessarily true —
  illustrates perceived-prestige, not just technical performance, as a
  real factor in material choice.
- **Concrete client-requirement examples** (Family Requirements /
  Preferences): a laundry hamper split into 4 separate sub-baskets for
  pre-sorting by category, in a dedicated laundry-adjacent space; two
  fully separate his-and-hers walk-in closets rather than one shared
  closet; a primary bathroom with two full sinks, private bedroom access,
  hygienic shower, and separate toilet.

### Materials — Concentrated Primer Dilution Ratios + Sprayer Application Technique (added 2026-08-17)

Multi-speaker technical explainer with a named materials-company
technologist; no pricing. [source: yt_JyzPLUbp5YI]

- **Why prime a ceiling/surface before finishing**: binds accumulated dust
  (prevents later shedding through a stretch-ceiling membrane), reduces
  paint absorbency (prevents color show-through under a stretch ceiling),
  and improves adhesion for subsequent coatings — stated as a mandatory
  minimum step post-demolition, not optional.
- **Concentrated primer dilution ratios (concentrate:water), one product
  covering every priming use case in a renovation**: 1:1 — adhesion
  primer for non-absorbent surfaces; 1:3 — floor primer sized for good
  self-leveling-compound spread/bond; 1:5–1:6 — general strengthening
  primer (walls); 1:7–1:8 — deep-penetration primer (ceilings); 1:10 plus
  mixed into a compound (replacing ~8-10% of the mix's water) — turns the
  concentrate into a latex additive that measurably improves even
  inexpensive base materials' properties. **Caution**: a 10L concentrate
  canister is a meaningful cost outlay and is uneconomical for a single
  small room — it pays off at whole-apartment/multi-room scale where
  several dilution ratios are actually needed across a project.
- **Ceiling-priming application: garden sprayer beats roller**, demonstrated
  side by side — a 5-10L garden pump sprayer with diluted primer covers a
  full ceiling including corners in ~15 minutes with one person, versus a
  roller method (two roller sizes + extension poles + corner roller +
  corner brushes + primer bucket + full PPE) needing two people and taking
  longer for only partial coverage in the same demo. Sprayer advantages:
  less wasted primer (no floor drips/pooling), reliable coverage of
  hard-to-reach corners a brush/roller struggles with. **Apply liberally
  until the surface visibly can't absorb more ("until it starts to
  pool")** — not a fixed coverage rate.

### Walls / Ceilings — Self-Leveling Floor Technique (third playlist batch, added 2026-08-17)

Misleading "how not to" title again hides a positive technique demo — fifth instance of this pattern noted in this batch overall (fourth on self-leveling/masonry/window content specifically).

- **Room-zone partitioning before pour**: divide a large area into smaller zones using metal profile strips across doorways/openings, because self-leveling compound cures fast enough that a very large single pour risks the near side setting before the far side is reached, preventing a uniformly flat surface. `confirmed`. [source: yt_awsVIxZlskQ]
- **Edge tape (кромочная лента) around each zone's perimeter compensates for the screed's shrinkage/moisture-loss deformation while curing** — prevents the screed tearing itself apart as it dries. `confirmed`, genuinely new mechanism explanation for edge tape not previously recorded this specifically in this store. [source: yt_awsVIxZlskQ]
- **"Find the highest point, then work down from it" leveling method**: set a self-leveling rotary laser to horizontal mode for one continuous reference plane; tape-measure down to the floor at multiple points to find the room's highest point (smallest measured distance); raise the laser so its beam sits exactly 1 meter above that point; then use a metal template/gauge whose reference mark sits **995 mm from its bottom edge (1 m minus 5 mm)** to transfer a target pour-fill line around the room, 5 mm above the highest point everywhere. Pour and spread with a needle roller to the marked line in each zone — all zones join up level since every mark derives from the same laser plane. `confirmed`, a fully specified, checkable technique; the 995mm-template trick is a genuinely new precise numeric method for this store. [source: yt_awsVIxZlskQ]

### Flooring / Cost Drivers — Screed Zero-Reference Lowering Technique (third playlist batch, added 2026-08-17)

`yt_VrfCLF74L50` — "КАК НЕЛЬЗЯ ДЕЛАТЬ СТЯЖКУ ПОЛА #033", dense positive screed technique + cost walkthrough (2019-01-21, upload-date-confirmed). **First source in this batch with real pricing content** — 2019 Moscow RUB, `single-account`/`unverified`.

- **Laser-leveling zero-reference propagation method**: place a self-leveling laser near a room's center at any height on its first setup (only a virtual horizontal plane matters, not absolute height) and mark that plane on every wall it reaches; for rooms it can't directly reach, re-level to an already-marked point and propagate the same plane onward — one continuous reference plane through the whole apartment. Use an adjustable-rod tripod, not a plain one (fine height tuning by leg length alone is impractical).
- **"Zero" = the apartment's single highest raw-floor point** (found by measuring down from the laser plane at many points — smallest distance wins); every other point is recorded as a negative offset from it. Explicitly disambiguated from the unrelated construction-industry "zero mark" term.
- **Average raw-floor height variation across ~100 sampled new-build apartments: ~7cm for a ~100m² unit**, observed range 3-20cm. `unverified`, no methodology given, but a specific planning-sanity-check figure.
- **Minimum cement-sand screed thickness: 3cm, a hard floor** — thinner risks the screed crumbling/detaching.
- **Quantity/cost formula, worked example (100m² apartment, 7cm variation, 6.5cm average thickness)**: 1 bag (25kg) covers ~1m² at ~2cm → ~3 bags/m² at 6.5cm → ~320 bags (~13 tonnes). **2019 Moscow prices**: ~115 RUB/bag (~37,000 RUB material) + ~4,000-10,000 RUB delivery + ~1,500 RUB/tonne carry-up (~19,500 RUB) ≈ **~65,000 RUB total** (materials+delivery+carry, excludes labor).
- **Entrance-threshold level-matching rule**: finished floor at the entrance must match the building corridor level. If the corridor was raised significantly (up to ~15cm seen in some new-builds) above the apartment's raw floor, use lightweight expanded-clay fill, not pure screed, to close that gap economically — most units only see a 2-4cm gap where the lowering technique below applies instead.
- **Core cost-saving technique: deliberately lower the zero reference ~2cm below the true highest point**, screed only down from the new lower datum (respecting the 3cm minimum), and fill the small remaining wedge with thin self-leveling compound (a few mm, no 3cm minimum) instead of full-depth screed. A 2cm level difference across more than half a room's floor is stated as not visually detectable once flooring is down. **Two independently worked examples, same order of magnitude**: a real 140m² project (raw-floor range 0 to -8/-9cm, entrance ~-4.5cm) saved ~140 bags (~30,000 RUB) and avoided an entrance-threshold step defect; a virtual 100m² example (3cm thickness reduction) saved ~150 bags (~30,000-35,000 RUB) net of a ~10,000 RUB self-leveling-compound premium.
- **Screed vs. self-leveling compound — three distinguishing characteristics, with explained mechanism (not just asserted)**: screed needs beacons/manual leveling by a worker vs. self-leveling compound's own fluidity/gravity self-leveling; self-leveling compound is a finer, higher-strength mix resisting cracking at very thin layers where screed cannot go below 3cm; self-leveling compound costs significantly more per unit thickness, so it's used as a thin finishing/leveling pass (including atop any cement-sand screed, however well executed) rather than a full-depth fill.
- **Electrical-conduit-routing cost mechanism, extending this store's existing ceiling-not-floor rule** (`yt_Onu15qOeWGA`): a floor-routed low-voltage/data cable bundle (often ≥5cm thick) forces a local 10cm minimum screed buildup (5cm bundle + 5cm cover) — on the 140m² project's own numbers, this would have turned a 2cm entrance-threshold gap into an 8cm-taller defect. **Cost claim: an estimated extra ~120,000 RUB in avoidable screed volume** if that project had routed electrical on the floor instead of the ceiling.


### Kitchen / Bathroom / Bedroom / Entrance / Materials — "29 Stupidly Expensive Mistakes" Dense Dunk-Format Batch (second title-skim batch, added 2026-08-17)

Despite the channel's usual low-yield dunk format (per the prior trial), this specific video's rapid-fire "mistake → correct Zemstandart rule" structure surfaced ~20 distinct checkable rules. All `single-account`/`confirmed` as this company's stated standard unless noted; the critiqued designer/project itself is never named or independently verifiable.

- **Kitchen flooring must run along the main direction of foot traffic, or (if conflicting) along the direction of daylight entry — never crossing both.** Crossing the light direction visually emphasizes wear; crossing the traffic direction accelerates it. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Outlet height above kitchen countertop: 110cm from finished floor (Zemstandart standard), with a stated mechanism**: most appliance plugs sit at 90° to their cable, so an outlet placed too close to the countertop (example given: 95cm, only 5cm clearance) forces the plug in upside-down to clear the counter — a practical daily-use issue, not just aesthetic. Corroborates this store's existing 110cm figure with new supporting reasoning. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Kitchen flooring should run as a single unbroken contour, not split at a 45° diagonal seam mid-room** — a diagonal seam produces more offcut waste and is more likely to run across both the light and traffic directions simultaneously. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Built-in refrigerator preferred over freestanding by default** — freestanding leaves an open dust-trapping gap above the fridge and ill-fitting seams against the backsplash/countertop. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Flooring transitions between rooms should use a cork compensator strip, not a surface-mounted transition strip/threshold** — a surface strip protrudes above the floor plane, doesn't seal tightly along its length (trapping hard-to-remove dirt), and eventually lifts at its tip into a snagging/tripping hazard. Genuinely new flooring-transition rule for this store. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **A wavy/curved decorative wall surface is incompatible with standard baseboard and ceiling-cornice installation** — installers cannot fit straight trim against a non-planar wall, leaving a permanent hard-to-clean junction gap along its full length. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Stretch-ceiling baguette/trim profile must be consistent around a room's full perimeter** — worked QC-failure example: most of a kitchen ceiling got the more expensive "shadow-gap" profile, but one section near a window got a standard profile by mistake, despite paid "author supervision." [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Kitchen backsplash material should match the countertop material** — porcelain-tile backsplash flagged as outdated: grout lines yellow/degrade over time, grease can't be fully cleaned off, and there's no clean junction detail against the fridge or countertop. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Cooking-zone task lighting and general/overhead lighting are both mandatory in a kitchen, never optional or substituted by decorative/accent lighting alone** — worked examples: a project that omitted task lighting entirely can only add it later by disassembling half the kitchen (and had already paid for a now-nonfunctional switch/chase-cut for it); a kitchen with only a dim decorative dining fixture left the countertop nearly unlit, demonstrated on camera as a real finger-cutting safety risk. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Every kitchen outlet should have a defined purpose — no unassigned "spare" outlets** — each unnecessary outlet costs money three times over (purchase, installation labor, and any added cutout-drilling cost through tile). Worked example: six outlets clustered with no stated purpose for most of them. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Kitchen entry/passage clearance should be planned for two people passing simultaneously** — worked example: an 80cm passage showed visible baseboard-corner wear/damage despite the renovation being recent, circumstantial evidence of repeated bumping. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Bathtub/sink/toilet placement across candidate rooms should minimize (not maximize) the tightest workable end-clearance gap, with shortest plumbing run as a secondary tie-breaker** — worked example (1.70m tub): 30cm and 20cm end gaps both rejected in favor of a 12cm gap with the shortest fixture supply/drain run. Independently corroborated by the same-batch `yt_RDOUfxTW2sA`'s identical example, suggesting a standard company decision heuristic rather than incidental to one video. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Concealed-cistern installation frame requires dedicated wall-cavity space planned into the layout up front** — worked example: a DIY layout positioned toilets correctly relative to stack risers but omitted the wall box needed for the cistern/frame, requiring wall rework regardless of the rest of the layout. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Utility storage cabinet depth should match its contents, not a single default depth**: 600mm for small hand tools vs. 700mm for a full-size vacuum/buckets/mops — the 700mm size specifically needs real floor space a narrow leftover corridor can't supply. Extends this store's existing depth-by-purpose framework (previously 50cm technical/60cm outerwear, `yt_K-W4dLJGEtg`) with a new pairing. Independently corroborated by the same-batch `yt_RDOUfxTW2sA`. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Glass partition between a living room and bedroom provides effectively zero acoustic or visual privacy** — worked example: a "look nice" glass partition leaves the living room able to see directly into the bedroom, eliminating personal privacy for the bedroom's user. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Bedroom main-light switch must sit on the door-handle side, reachable without looking on entry, and must be a two-way ("passage") switch operable from both the entry and the bed** — worked counter-examples for both: a switch requiring the occupant to first locate it then reach behind the open door, and a single-point switch forcing the occupant to get up and walk to the door to turn off the main light after getting into bed. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Curtain zone must be planned before finalizing nearby electrical point positions, not after** — worked example: bedside outlets/switches and a fixture positioned without accounting for curtain travel, so drawn curtains physically block the fixture and the affected outlet/switch. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Passage-switch requirement extends to hallways/entryways connecting to a kitchen** — worked example: an omitted hallway passage-switch forces re-crossing the "dirty" shoe-removal entry zone just to turn off the hallway light after walking through to the kitchen. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Electric towel warmers are the only "modern renovation" recommendation; water-fed towel warmers are called outdated** because they will eventually leak — worked example additionally lacked any leak-sensor protection. Corroborates existing electric-vs-hydronic towel-warmer content in this store (`yt_nFWxi3zbsNo`). [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Ceramic/stone hearth-protection material should only surround a genuine wood/gas fireplace, never an electric fireplace** — worked example: porcelain-tile fire-protection surface installed around an electric fireplace added cost with no functional benefit and created an unnecessary flooring-seam trip hazard. Genuinely new rule for this store. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Concealed/hidden doors require matching concealed baseboards, never a standard visible baseboard profile.** [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **A freestanding/vessel bathroom sink not integrated with its countertop requires ongoing maintenance**: water spots/splashes/grime accumulate on the surrounding countertop (can't be wiped into the basin the way an integrated sink allows) and dirt collects in the gap underneath. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]
- **Bathtub must be installed before wall tiling is completed, not after**, so tile can overhang the tub edge for a sealed junction — worked counter-example: tub installed after tiling required a visible trim/baseboard strip with sealant that discolors over time and needs periodic renewal. Genuinely new sequencing rule for this store. [source: yt_WVfgE0lHgVc]

### Multi-Trade QC Checklist — "Never Accept This From Tradesmen" (added 2026-08-17)

Dense on-location critique of an unrelated rental-apartment renovation
(not the speaker's own project or a client's) — a curated highest-value
subset of a much longer list; several minor QC-anecdote items were not
individually captured (see the source note's own Gaps section). No
pricing content. **Two items cross-reference other sources processed in
this same batch** (flagged inline). [source: yt_s5Gyi8Rd9D0]

- **Never caulk over the natural expansion gap between a door frame and
  its slope panel with matching-color sealant** — the thin dark line is a
  deliberate movement joint; filling it solid looks tidier short-term but
  cracks again as materials move.
- **Decorative floor-material insert accents are a planning mistake, not
  a design win** — virtually always end up covered by a rug + furniture,
  permanently hiding the accent while the owner still paid for expensive
  fitted offcuts. Default to full uniform-coverage flooring instead.
- **Always build a wardrobe/closet flush to the ceiling and flush with
  the adjacent wall face** — a ceiling gap is a permanent dust/allergen
  collector invisible except by reaching up; a protruding corner is both
  ugly and a genuine collision hazard.
- **Pre-check a built-in wardrobe's footprint against an adjacent door's
  full swing clearance before framing**, not just its static opening
  position — a real mistake shown had a wardrobe obstruct normal door
  operation. **Sliding-wardrobe-door design must also account for both
  positions of any adjacent hinged door**, so the hinged door's swing
  never strikes the sliding panel.
- **Never substitute a curtain for a proper door/opening on a closet off
  a narrow hallway** — a real collision-into-exposed-structural-corner
  hazard. Use a centered swing-in door, an offset door forming an
  L-shaped closet, or double sliding doors — **sliding doors only when
  the opening is wider than ~175cm** (otherwise a panel's parked end is
  visibly intrusive). `confirmed`, new numeric threshold for this store.
- **Kitchen cook-zone electrical-point spec**: the primary cook zone
  (between sink and cooktop) needs "3 electro-points" = 2 sockets + a
  lighting switch, not 3 full sockets; a secondary/auxiliary prep zone
  gets 3 full sockets.
- **Countertop-height vs. window-sill-height conflict**: don't raise an
  entire counter run to match a ~110cm sill at the cost of the
  ergonomic ~90cm counter height — fix counter height for comfort first,
  then lower the sill to match if the facade design allows it.
- **Countertop condensation-relief holes near a window only work if
  centered over the glass unit and correctly aimed**, otherwise purely
  decorative. **Cross-reference**: corroborates/extends `yt_qwIRfgn1Tog`'s
  DIY windowsill-vent-drilling fix (same batch) — same underlying
  mechanism from the design/build side vs. the homeowner-retrofit side.
- **Route a kitchen's water shutoff valve/riser access into the WC/
  bathroom rather than behind an in-kitchen wall hatch** — avoids a hatch
  frequently installed backwards or otherwise obtrusive in a finished
  kitchen. **Cross-reference**: complements `yt_PO9kd1cqCyA`'s utility-
  riser-access content (same batch, different context — that source
  covers a commercial project's riser-boxing demolition).
- **Prefer a built-in (panel-integrated) refrigerator over freestanding**
  — a freestanding unit or a decorative niche around one leaves a
  dust-collecting gap with no compensating benefit.
- **Bathroom switch-usability pattern**: main-light switch at the doorway
  (hit naturally on the way out); a separate exhaust-fan switch near the
  toilet/sink (so a user doesn't need to stand and reach the door to
  trigger it) — never place the fan switch outside the bathroom itself.
- **The "gap under the tub for your feet" convention is explicitly
  obsolete** — tile should run flush to the floor line under the tub's
  front edge, sealed with grout, no reachable gap. A tile "step" ridge
  insert marketed as space-saving has no functional benefit either — just
  a dirt/grime collector.
- **Reject an unexplained "always 3 sockets near the sink" rule of
  thumb** — size actual socket count to genuine need instead.
- **Bedroom lighting-temperature consistency rule**: use at most 2-3
  lighting circuits per room, and ensure every fixture across those
  circuits shares the same color temperature — mixing temperatures across
  multiple circuits reads as visually chaotic. Extends this store's
  existing three-layer-lighting concept (`yt_OCjVd5HOcac`) with a
  concrete circuit-count ceiling and explicit same-temperature
  constraint.
- **Window sash sizing must account for the finished floor height before
  the window order is placed** — the sash's lowest swing position must
  clear the finished floor covering with margin, or a sagging sash will
  eventually scrape it. New sequencing rule (order-of-operations across
  two trades).
- **A tilt-and-turn sash's scissor hardware can be damaged by improper
  closing technique** (opening past the scissor-disengagement point, then
  pushing it not-quite-closed causes repeated impact damage to the slope)
  — a misuse-driven failure mode distinct from this store's existing
  manufacturing-defect mechanisms for tilt-and-turn hardware
  (`yt_XFrmhhM1ogg`).

### Kids Room (new topic area, added 2026-08-14)

- **Avoid the phrase "детский дом" (children's home/orphanage) in client-facing language for a kids'-house/loft-bed structure — use "детский домик" instead.** `confirmed`, `single-account`. [source: yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU]
- **A kids'-house/loft-bed structure (детский домик) must be planned and wired into finish electrical from the start of a renovation, not added after** — it's described as too tightly integrated with finish electrical (independent switches at each sleeping spot) to retrofit cleanly once the rest of the renovation is complete. `confirmed`, `single-account`, a specific sequencing rule. [source: yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU]
- **Provision two independently-controlled switches near each sleeping spot in a shared kids' room**: one pass-through switch for the room's main light, one separate switch for a personal night-light — avoids one child needing to negotiate with a sibling over the shared main light. `confirmed`, demonstrated in the company's own project. [source: yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU]
- **Deliberately leave the middle section of a shared two-child wardrobe unused as a neutral buffer zone between each child's own storage area** — an explicit, acknowledged storage-capacity tradeoff made specifically to reduce recurring sibling territorial conflict. `confirmed` as stated design rationale, `single-account`. [source: yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU]
- **When building a wardrobe with an intentionally-unused buffer section, add its dedicated interior-lighting electrical cable during the same install** — stated as impossible to add cleanly once the wardrobe itself is installed, extending the general "wire before you build" theme also present elsewhere in this channel's content. `confirmed`, `single-account`. [source: yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU]

### Smart Home / Switch-Logic — "Stupidest Smart House" Critique (new topic area, added 2026-08-14)

- **Switches should be positioned so their function is inferable purely by physical proximity/logic**, without a printed legend — demonstrated on camera with an unrehearsed test: a staff designer correctly guessed which of four switches controlled which of four light fixtures purely from layout logic, with no prior briefing. `confirmed`, directly demonstrated. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Multi-button "piano"-style scene-control switch panels (up to ~64-68 preset scenes) are explicitly scoped by this company to business-class-and-above budgets, not comfort-class** — framed as disproportionately complex/expensive relative to benefit at the comfort price point; comfort-class default is individual, logically-placed switches instead. **New tier-scoping nuance** on switch/control hardware choice, distinct from this store's existing physical-placement/wiring switch rules. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **"Smart door" is not a distinct product — buy a plain door and a separate smart lock instead of a pre-integrated bundle**, reported to save "at least 3x." Regardless of price tier, fingerprint/app smart locks are reported to occasionally fail to open reliably; the phone-app fallback is reported to work in practice. `confirmed` as stated company advice, `single-account`. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Demonstrated voice-assistant (Alice/Yandex) command-batching limitation**: a single combined command covering two actions ("open the curtains and turn off the light") reliably failed to execute either action in a repeated on-camera test; the same two actions issued as separate sequential commands worked. A partial-position curtain command ("close 50%") also worked. `confirmed`, directly demonstrated — genuinely new voice-assistant reliability caveat for this store. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Rule: any smart/voice-controlled lighting circuit must retain a parallel physical switch** — without one, a malfunction forces using the branch breaker in the electrical panel as the only fallback. `confirmed`, reinforces this store's existing physical-switch-duplication principle from an independent source. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **"Duty/night light" (дежурное освещение) — continuous low-level lighting kept on in hallways and bathrooms/WCs for night-time use, without needing full lighting.** Explicit search-term note from the speaker: "duty light" is internal company jargon and won't find the product online — search "подсветка лестницы" (stair lighting) instead to find the correct fixture type. `confirmed`, checkable sourcing tip. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Milled/routed cabinet and door handles preferred over push-pull mechanisms (unreliable release, demonstrated on camera) and over protruding standard handles (snag/damage adjacent surfaces on swing-open, real collision-injury risk)** — stated as this company's now-default choice. `confirmed` as company stated preference with mechanism given, `single-account`. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Low-voltage/server cabinet or panel must include ventilation** or accumulated equipment heat risks failure over time — never buy furniture with a built-in ventilation grille for this (≈3x markup vs. a plain cabinet + a separately-sourced generic grille cut in by an installer). `confirmed` as stated design rule/purchasing tactic, `single-account`. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Ultra-thin/flush-mount TVs use a single combined power+signal UTP connection, locking the TV type into the design-project stage before rough-in is finalized** — if the buyer later switches to a standard TV, the wall's single UTP outlet cannot supply a standard TV's normal power+signal inputs; described as not reversible once walls are closed. Ultra-thin TVs quoted at roughly 3x a comparable standard TV's price. `confirmed` mechanism, `single-account`, `unverified` price multiple. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Comfort/business/elite labor-only price bands restated (not a new corroborating data point)**: comfort 25,000-45,000 / business 50,000-85,000 / elite 90,000-150,000 RUB/m², materials excluded — identical to the figures already recorded from `yt_7VfZIYGUrTo` and `yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU`, all same company within the same week. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]

### "Best Solutions" Mixed-Topic Compilation Batch (playlist `PLpZakfG8U02uR3KlsxtWXyChOETu-o-Aj`, added 2026-08-15)

All items below are `single-account` (same channel) and `ASR-uncertain` unless noted, given this sub-cluster's generally degraded transcript quality relative to most sources already in this store.

- **Wi-Fi access-point placement in reinforced-concrete apartments**: reinforced-concrete interior walls significantly attenuate Wi-Fi signal; a large apartment is described as needing roughly 3 access points for reliable whole-unit coverage, with at least one hidden inside a ceiling/soffit detail rather than left exposed. **Genuinely new topic for this store** — no prior source addresses networking/Wi-Fi equipment placement. `ASR-uncertain`, treat the "3 access points" figure as one rough worked example, not a general rule. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Dedicated circuit for network/router equipment**, with an accessible point to power-cycle it. `ASR-uncertain`, mechanism unclear. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Dedicated circuit for a child's room** to avoid overloading a single outlet/circuit. `ASR-uncertain`. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Lost-key cost-saving tip**: replacing only the lock cylinder, not the whole lock/hardware set, restores security cheaply. No price given. `ASR-uncertain`. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Mirror lighting should match the room's main light source's color temperature**, so a person's reflected appearance matches how they'll look under the room's general lighting. `ASR-uncertain`. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Closet/wardrobe interior shelf depth rule of thumb**: roughly 40-50 cm. Reasoning not reconstructable from this transcript. `ASR-uncertain`, treat as approximate. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Bathroom grab bar/safety rail near the shower or tub** to prevent slip injuries — general safety point, no installation detail recoverable. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Kitchen cooking-zone sightline rule**: orient the work zone so the person cooking can turn and see the room without fully turning their back to the stove. `ASR-uncertain`, mechanism not fully reconstructable. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Single-loop ("единым контуром") underfloor heating under laminate flooring carries a roughly 50/50 buckling-failure risk**, per the practitioner's own stated recent-years experience — quartz-vinyl tile mitigates but doesn't eliminate the risk, especially in irregularly-shaped rooms. **Safety/defect-relevant, genuinely new explicit failure-rate heuristic for this store.** `confirmed` as stated practitioner risk assessment, `single-account`, `unverified` as an industry statistic. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Voltage-protection relay (реле напряжения), now recommended on every panel**: one relay per phase (1 for single-phase service, 3 for three-phase). [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Server/network closet convention**: incoming twisted-pair, fiber, and TV-signal cabling all terminate in one small closet/panel housing the TV patch panel, router, and NAS, then redistribute to the whole apartment from there. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Electric towel warmers preferred over hydronic (water-fed) ones "without genuine necessity"** — stated rationale: trivial electricity cost vs. near-certain eventual water-line leak and its repair cost to the unit and the unit below. `single-account` stated practice/opinion. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Always pair a hygiene shower/bidet with at least a small sink**, even in a tight bathroom, so hands can be washed without touching a dirty door handle or walking to another room's sink. Genuinely new pairing rule for this store. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Avoid installed audio wall-outlet plates for speaker wiring — leave capped bare wire stubs behind the wall instead**, so removed speakers don't leave a permanent visible unused outlet; extra connector pairs also mildly degrade audio signal quality. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Paintable, texture-free wallpaper ("обои под покраску") allows invisible local spot-repainting** (even paint absorption vs. a painted-plaster wall's visible patch marks) — also the correct product choice for a Venetian-plaster *look*, using a wallpaper that imitates the texture rather than real plaster. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Trim all openings/arches/portals — including a joined loggia's reveal — with interior-door casing extensions ("доборы"), never natural stone/brick veneer**, which doesn't join cleanly with a standard baseboard. Cited cost ≈5x a ~2,000 RUB PVC-corner-trim baseline, still judged good value for the visual-quality gain. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Vacuum-cleaner outlet mounting height ≈1.1 m** — avoids bending to plug in, and the cord tends to release cleanly rather than yank the plug when dragged, versus a floor-level outlet. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Ethernet run to every TV location is standard regardless of Wi-Fi**, because Wi-Fi is not considered reliable enough for consistently high-quality video streaming. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Switch must be on the same side of a doorway as the door handle** — stated as a firm, non-negotiable ergonomic rule, with a real on-camera correction example of a misplaced switch. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Furniture assembly is outside a renovation contractor's standard scope; baseboard/trim fitting around furniture and built-in kitchen IS standard scope**, performed after furniture placement — a useful scope-of-work boundary for a self-managed project sequencing its own trades. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Socket-to-sink minimum-distance concern framed by the practitioner as a largely outdated myth for modern IP-rated sockets** (vs. genuinely poor 1960s aluminum-contact sockets) — `single-account`, `unverified` electrical-safety opinion, not a cited code standard; do not adopt as verified fact. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **A kitchen niche will almost always become an appliance "parking spot" (multicooker/kettle/coffee maker) regardless of its intended storage use** — default to adding a couple of outlets to any kitchen niche. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Never use polypropylene water-supply pipe — use standard plastic pipe with reliable fittings instead.** Firm, repeated practitioner position, justified anecdotally by claimed repeated on-camera rework of failed polypropylene systems. `single-account`, `unverified` industry-wide claim (no failure mechanism/standard cited) — genuinely new pipe-material warning for this store. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Factory-assembled, pre-tested electrical panels preferred over field-assembled ones**: factory units are load-tested, defective units are culled, and they ship with a manufacturer's warranty plus a product passport/certificate of conformity — none available from an on-site-assembled panel. Genuinely new panel-sourcing quality rule, complementary to this store's existing hired-electrician vetting signals. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Shoe-dryer outlets**: install two outlets near shoe storage specifically for electric shoe-dryer inserts. Genuinely new appliance-specific outlet-provisioning rule. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Server-closet circuit-separation detail** (extends the `yt_nFWxi3zbsNo` server-closet convention): of 4 outlets in the closet, 3 share one circuit and the 4th (dedicated to the NAS) is on a separate circuit, so rebooting the router doesn't also reboot network-attached storage. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Leak-detection sensor should sit a few centimeters above true floor level and never be glued down**, so it can be dried with a hairdryer and easily restored to service after a flooding event rather than pried up. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Windowsill-drilled directional-vent retrofit for window condensation**: if condensation persists after wide-windowsill installation blocks radiator warm air from reaching the glass, drill a hole through the sill opposite the center of each glass unit and insert a directional grille to channel warm air onto the lower glass. New concrete retrofit technique, beyond this store's existing "installation quality is the primary condensation cause" heuristic. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Wall-panel (PVC/decorative cladding) corner-forming technique without separate corner trim**: cut a groove in the panel's back edge, then repeatedly heat the cut line and wipe before folding around interior corners (heating required); exterior corners can fold cold if enough backing material is removed during the cut. Saves cost/time versus separate corner-trim pieces. Genuinely new fabrication technique for this store. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Loggia climate/lighting controls should be mounted in the door reveal/opening, not deep inside the loggia** — faster access, avoids stepping onto a cold unheated floor to reach the control, and status is visible from the warm side. Extends this store's existing loggia-control-placement content with a specific ergonomic rationale. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Custom furniture sourced from the same supplier as the kitchen cabinetry, for stylistic cohesion** — costs more than off-the-shelf furniture. `tier-steering`: company recommending its own bespoke-sourcing option; a cheaper independently-sourced alternative exists for a self-managed buyer. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Prefer a patterned/speckled finish over a uniform dark/monotone color for a countertop or flooring surface** — pattern/contrast conceals dust, water spots, and footprints that a plain color shows every mark of; a real client case is cited where an imposed dark monotone countertop proved high-maintenance and is being replaced. Extends this store's existing flooring-pattern-hides-dirt principle to countertops. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Two separate, smaller (~260 L-class) refrigerators instead of one large unit**: cheaper than an equivalent-capacity single large fridge, allows food-category separation, and lets one unit be powered off entirely (e.g. while traveling) to save electricity. Genuinely new appliance-provisioning heuristic. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Microwave-over-oven appliance column should use lift-up (not hinged-down) cabinet doors** to avoid head-bumping when opened. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Cove/indirect ceiling lighting via a stretch-ceiling-integrated LED profile** (shaped edge profile with a translucent insert concealing the LED strip) replaces an open, dust-collecting light-strip channel; RGB/dimmable, ≈30,000 RUB added per room. Genuinely new lighting technique + price point, distinct from this store's existing (dust-exposed) perimeter-LED-baguette-channel content. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Remote/electronic entrance locks fail open (auto-unlock) when the battery becomes critically low**, as a stated safety default — meaning battery neglect is a real security exposure, not just an inconvenience. No ordinary lock (electronic or mechanical) resists a skilled break-in for more than ~30 seconds except a concealed lock, which can only be defeated by slow, loud mechanical grinding. Extends this store's existing smart-lock content with a security/reliability trade-off analysis. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Wardrobe/cabinet-box installer marking protocol to protect concealed wiring before a later stretch-ceiling install**: photograph the installed box, have the client independently photograph it, and attach a physical note stating the exact distance from the box's underside to the concealed cable run plus a maximum safe fastener length (35-40mm) — so a later stretch-ceiling crew doesn't drive a screw through hidden cabling. Genuinely new cross-trade coordination/documentation protocol. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Bathroom tile-finish selection heuristic**: choose a surface that hides water/soap spots but still shows toothpaste splatter — not so glossy every water droplet shows, not so matte grime disappears into the texture. Distinct from this store's existing tile-*layout* (cut-piece sizing) rules. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Hygienic-shower/bidet-sprayer plumbing without a wall shutoff valve, worked mechanism**: the sprayer head has no reliable shutoff hardware of its own, so constant line pressure risks an eventual burst/leak; shutoff instead routes through a button on the sprayer's wall-mounted holder (a "second circuit"), combined with a pre-set in-wall thermostatic mixing valve so no separate temperature control is needed at the sprayer. Trade-off: slightly less convenient docking/undocking. Genuinely new plumbing-mechanism detail, distinct from this store's existing hygienic-shower handedness-placement fact. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Joining a loggia increases total heat-loss surface area versus leaving it unjoined**, so the combined room runs somewhat cooler — fully compensable with normal underfloor heating and building heating, not a reason to avoid joining. Extends this store's existing loggia-join heat-loss content. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Renovation-budget spending-sequencing rule**: fully fund and complete concealed/"fundamental" work first (wiring, plumbing, tile, windows), since redoing any of it later is far costlier than doing it right the first time; defer non-structural, purchasable-later items instead (stretch ceilings can be added weeks/months post-move-in, door casings and doors themselves can wait until last, a kitchen can be delayed up to roughly six months). Distinct from this store's existing "don't cut cost on concealed systems" heuristic (`yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw`) — adds the *timing/sequencing* dimension. `single-account`. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Bathtub protection during rough work: plywood over the tub, rim masked with several layers of painter's tape underneath** — not just plastic sheeting; a real workaround warning is also recorded: crews sometimes flip dirty plywood over to look clean for a client/inspector visit rather than replace it, so the taped rim protects the tub even when the visible plywood has been "gamed." [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Floor drain/duct outlet grilles must be installed strictly level** — an uneven grille conflicts with surrounding flooring installation and looks/feels wrong underfoot. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Bathtub rim must sit recessed 1.5-3cm below the adjacent tile/wall surface, never flush** — a flush installation degrades the grout/sealant line faster over time. Genuinely new numeric installation rule. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Relief/textured tile forces a trade-off with perfectly mitered 45° inside corners** — no installer achieves a truly clean inside corner with relief-textured tile; choose either perfect corners or textured tile, not both. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **The structural ceiling behind a stretch-ceiling fabric must always be painted** (any color) so nothing sheds onto the underside of the stretch-ceiling fabric over time. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **On-site CCTV is installed to supervise crew behavior, not as a client-facing feature** — crews reportedly work more carefully and professionally when filmed; footage also protects workers from later disputed blame. `single-account`. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Plumbing access-hatch design principle**: sized/positioned for full "revision" access so any single fitting/valve can be removed and replaced individually without disassembling the surrounding assembly — not a decorative feature. Extends this store's general utility-concealment-box sizing heuristic with mechanism detail. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **A full supply+exhaust ventilation unit can consume a large share of ceiling height**, often forcing partial ceiling drops in adjacent rooms too — budget for lost height/cost in more than one room. **Sequence duct-routing subcontractors before finalizing or photographing planned electrical wiring layouts** — ad hoc duct routing reportedly overrides an already-neat electrical layout regardless of planning. Genuinely new cost/space trade-off + cross-trade sequencing rule. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **A genuine "tropical"/rain shower head must protrude from the wall on a proper riser arm or be ceiling-mounted** — a horizontal hand-held-style hose connection is not a real tropical-shower installation. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **To-the-ceiling kitchen cabinetry combined with a stretch ceiling needs a purpose-built corner box** (sized to the cabinetry footprint, sometimes with integrated upper-cabinet lighting) so the stretch-ceiling baguette can attach to the box's edge while the kitchen mounts to/near the same box — extends this store's existing wardrobe-ceiling-box coordination principle to a second furniture type. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Pre-wire a dedicated outlet for a heated-seat/smart toilet before finish work**, rather than improvising a connection from a nearby sink outlet afterward. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Never change a door/opening height mid-project once construction has started** — real cost comparison: reworking apartment-wide openings costs ≈2 extra days with paintable wallpaper finish, but ≈2 extra *weeks* with painted walls or standard wallpaper. Reinforces this store's existing paintable-wallpaper preference from a rework-cost angle. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Coordinate entrance-door and interior-door trim material at time of purchase** — different manufacturers make each, so a mismatch is easy unless deliberately specified together up front. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Real regret case: cutting a designed built-in washing-machine countertop to save ≈15,000 RUB later cost ≈50,000 RUB to retrofit.** Concrete number reinforcing this store's design-integrity-vs-cost-cutting theme. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Every entryway needs a dedicated, separately-lit makeup-mirror zone**, distinct from a general full-length mirror (not adequately lit for grooming checks). [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Floor-drain slope-direction rule when the drain is offset from the wall**: slope toward the drain from both directions, not one uniform slope — a uniform slope leaves a low point away from the drain where water pools and mold forms. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Budget lifehack: hide network/server equipment in a cheap small wall-mounted box as an interim solution** before a proper built-in server-closet cabinet is affordable. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Window-desk warm-airflow eye-strain mechanism**: a convector's warm air blown into a seated person's face at a window desk dries the eyes and accelerates eyesight strain — a third reason (alongside condensation and constant daylight) to only build a window desk as a last resort. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Windowsill-desk height must be lowered to match a standard chair, with electrical points planned for that lowered position** — real regret case: a client cancelled the lowered-desk millwork mid-project without telling the designer, leaving pre-planned outlets visibly misplaced relative to the simplified windowsill actually built. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Frameless glass shower-divider real cleaning-time data point**: ≈1.5-2 hours to clean fingerprint/water streaks off one panel on move-in day, including an improvised cleaning tool for an inaccessible corner seam — concrete maintenance-burden evidence for the glass-vs-curtain shower-divider trade-off. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Hallway/passage switch topology, worked example**: a passage connecting 3 rooms needs at least 3 separate switches so no one has to backtrack to turn off a light behind them. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Never add a pass-through switch immediately outside a bathroom that already has its own 2 switches (main + duty light)** — unintuitive (flipped behind the person leaving) and risks confusing the switch group. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **A bathroom/WC needs its own duty/night light, not just the connecting hallway's** — cover all rooms needing duty lighting on one shared switch. Extends this store's existing duty-light content with the WC-specific gap. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Track-lighting (шинопровод) minimum busbar width for a room's main light source: 38mm** — most designers spec 20-25mm rails, which can only carry weaker fixtures inadequate as primary lighting. First track-lighting content in this store. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Avoid a light fixture with 3 fixed cycled color-temperature presets via repeated clicking** — every use requires cycling through unwanted tones; prefer a single-tone, single-mode fixture instead. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Unplanned late loggia join without 3D pre-modeling causes compounding junction defects, real case**: kitchen cabinetry met the new opening trim with zero gap (should have been offset ~40mm); the loggia-side countertop needed 3 recuts for not accounting for wall-cladding and trim thickness. **Numeric rule: a countertop must overhang the adjoining wall/trim assembly by a minimum of 40mm** to correctly seat trim up to ~20mm thick without appearing to float. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Bathroom leak-sensor count by floor-level choice**: a lowered bathroom floor needs at least 1 leak sensor; a level (flush) floor needs 2, since there's no natural low point guaranteeing single-sensor coverage. Extends this store's existing leak-sensor content. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Laminate-under-underfloor-heating risk, refined**: even in a small apartment, split laminate into separate zones/loops per room rather than one continuous loop, regardless of apartment size — quartz-vinyl remains safe in one continuous loop. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Curtain-pelmet channel + shadow-gap stretch-ceiling combination technique**: a specialized recessed curtain-rod pelmet channel that the stretch-ceiling fabric deliberately stops short of, so curtain-rod concealment and a shadow-gap "floating ceiling" effect (otherwise mutually exclusive) can coexist. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Vet a stretch-ceiling contractor by whether they leave access hatches near track-lighting transformers/drivers** — these fail periodically regardless of price tier; no access panel means full ceiling demolition/reinstall for every future repair (effectively a recurring "subscription fee"). [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Joining a loggia with flush flooring usually requires raising the loggia floor 10-15cm (≈90% of apartments)**, but occasionally the whole apartment's floor must be raised instead — real cited cost "several hundred thousand RUB," with a real downside: the finished floor can end up higher than the shared-corridor entry threshold, forcing a step down on exit. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Sliding-door (шкаф-купе) wardrobes are cheaper than framed/corpus wardrobes of the same footprint** — no back/top/bottom panel fabrication needed since the surrounding wall/niche does that job — plus more space-efficient and generally more reliable hardware. Always plan a ceiling box above a corpus wardrobe if chosen instead. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Temporary baseboard with pre-planned future cut zones**: install temp baseboard everywhere (even future-furniture walls) so the unit doesn't look unfinished during the year-plus gap before furniture arrives — but pre-mark the exact points a later furniture crew will need to cut, so they cut cleanly instead of tearing the baseboard/wallpaper at an arbitrary spot. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Plan TV-wall outlet position specifically behind the actual planned TV location**, not just generally on the TV wall — a real mistake shown produces visible cable "spaghetti" on day one. Extends this store's existing TV-cable-channel-provisioning content. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Kids-room door-swing exception (extends the store's existing ~95° default)**: an unusually wide ~180° swing is deliberate when a large wardrobe will occupy the space a normal door stop would use. Wardrobe interior lighting must always be planned in advance — frequently forgotten. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Fresh-air "breather" wall-placement trade-off, brand-agnostic**: surface-mounted near a window looks tech-forward but conflicts with curtain operation and wastes floor space; recessed into a technical niche avoids that but leaves permanently-visible imperfect exposed corners. Presented as the lesser evil vs. losing floor space. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Two-component ("двухкомпонентные") radiator grilles**: an outer frame plus a magnetically-attached center insert that pops off alone for access (bleeding air, wiping down), unlike a standard fixed grille that's very hard to remove. Made to order by the same custom-fabrication shops as standard bespoke grilles. Designer (exposed, never concealed) radiators cost roughly 3x a standard radiator. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Stretch-ceiling light lines can now fully replace a room's main/central light fixture** — bright enough for both use and cleaning per the practitioner — but cost significantly more than a standard fixture, and a failed light line can require replacing the stretch ceiling itself to repair. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Paintable wallpaper mechanism refinement**: because wallpaper absorbs some paint, the finished surface comes out slightly matte, hiding fingerprint/grease marks that show clearly on a directly-painted wall — a specific visual mechanism for this store's existing paintable-wallpaper-preference theme. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Wall-finish assignment (which covering on which wall) should be spelled out explicitly, wall-by-wall, in design-project documentation.** [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Laminated-finish window sills/slopes should match the window's laminate color, not default to white** — window manufacturers generally don't sell matching sill/slope panels, and matching-color panels are hard to find at typical building-materials markets; the fix is sourcing through specialized companies, not general retail. Repeatedly observed failure pattern: premium laminated windows paired with mismatched white sills/slopes. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Curtain cornices are now installed flush with, or slightly above, the ceiling plane** via a dedicated ceiling profile (previously hand-built from drywall), hiding mounting hardware above the curtain's top edge rather than leaving a cornice visibly hanging below the ceiling. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Second, upper electrical outlet inside a window slope**, in addition to a lower one, specifically for seasonal window/holiday light garlands — avoids visibly dangling cords from a low outlet; the upper outlet sits normally hidden behind closed curtains most of the year. Extends this store's existing window-slope-outlet content. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Quartz-vinyl (SPC) flooring used on nearly every project by this company, including budget tiers** — installs as one continuous field across room boundaries without transition strips at doorways; even a cheaper quartz-vinyl product line is stated as still water-resistant and seamless-installable, not just premium tiers. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Kitchen curtain necessity depends on whole-apartment window-covering consistency** — if every other room uses roller blinds rather than curtains, a kitchen with no curtain zone designed into the layout doesn't need one either; not a universal "never curtain a kitchen" rule. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Large fixed panoramic window panes that cannot be manually washed should be cleaned with a magnetic window-cleaning gadget (manual or motorized)**, not industrial climbers (expensive, inconsistent quality) or by ordering the sash split into operable sections — splitting a large fixed facade pane into opening sections is described as tearing hinges/sagging over time, creating a cold thermal bridge that ices over in winter, and **triggering a facade-code violation with a formal restoration order plus fines**. Genuinely new panoramic-glazing maintenance + facade-modification regulatory-risk warning. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Always pre-size a radiator recess/niche for a concealment grille even for a client who currently wants an open radiator** — clients commonly change their mind after living with an exposed radiator, but retrofitting a niche later is often impossible once walls are finished; pre-sizing costs little extra at design time. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Standard multi-hinge revision/access hatches sag measurably over time** — real example: an even ~2mm perimeter gap widened to 4mm on one edge (0mm/binding on the opposite edge) within about a week of installation, worsening irreversibly once it starts. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Avoid automatic/sensor-triggered fixtures (motion-activated exhaust fans, automatic lighting) in favor of simple manual on/off switching** — automatic triggers are described as uncontrollable, acting on their own sensor logic rather than user intent. `single-account` design philosophy. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Study/homework desk placement rule**: should sit strictly to the right of a window so daylight falls from the left onto the writing hand (classroom-layout model).
