Full detail in [[03_Kitchen/Kitchen_General|Kitchen: Layout & Configuration]] (new content for a previously-empty page). [source: yt_BUfY5YcnN-U]

- **Don't default to one continuous kitchen run on a large open kitchen-living space** — splitting cooking/prep and casual-bar-seating functions into separate modules on different walls can solve circulation, furniture-placement, and corner-protection problems a single continuous layout can't solve together. Worked example, reasoning by elimination: an island was rejected for four independent reasons (infeasible sink drainage without a noisy pump, cooking-odor/hood-height conflict with the room, an exposed corner plus no viable sofa/dining-table placement, and no real dining function since a cabinet-based island has no legroom underneath); a U-shape-with-bar-tongue was rejected for a ~4.5m detour path and no viable sofa placement; the chosen split-module layout solved every one of those problems simultaneously, plus incidentally protected the room's one exposed corner.

### Planning Rules — Irregular-Apartment Full Replan (Category 4 batch, chunk 4, added 2026-08-19)

Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_OUX-lc5Wqog_irregular_apartment_replanning_212|extraction note]]. [source: yt_OUX-lc5Wqog]

- **Developer non-structural partitions treated as position-only placeholders**, never a usable base — near-universally poorly built per this practitioner's stated experience, demolish and rebuild regardless of apparent position accuracy.
- **230mm minimum usable wall-segment depth**: below this, a segment can't even hold a wardrobe's own back panel — reallocate the wall rather than accept the compromise.
- **Kitchen-alcove depth should match the kitchen's own standard module depth (600mm)**, not an arbitrary larger box — an oversized alcove wastes floor area the cabinetry won't use.
- **1100mm door-opening width is insufficient for any standard interior door leaf**, and additionally needs reveal/return framing (отбортовка) a correct install requires — both defects require rebuilding the opening, not widening it.
- **900mm WC width shrinks to ~800mm once plastered/trued** — at 800mm only a toilet fits, not even the smallest sink; a genuine planning trap if the pre-plaster raw dimension is used for fixture-fit decisions.
- **Radiator false-wall depth by wall type, routed to [[12_Engineering_and_Systems/Heating|Heating]]**: ~50mm against an ordinary partition, a full ~150mm against a load-bearing wall (which can't be recessed into at all).

### Planning Rules — Screed-First vs. Walls-First Sequencing (Category 4 batch, chunk 4, added 2026-08-19)

Direct prequel to this store's existing zero-reference/working-reference content (`yt_VHfUDBr0p4Q`, chunk 2, explicit two-part series). Full detail in [[13_Surfaces_and_Finishes/Flooring_Guide|Flooring Guide]] (new section) and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_cJLZebMtW7A_screed_or_walls_first_108|extraction note]]. [source: yt_cJLZebMtW7A]

- **The deciding factor is wall construction material, not preference**: masonry walls (aerated/foam block) → walls first, screed after (volume-fill economics favor cheap block over screed under the future wall footprint — 1-2m³ difference on a 100m² apartment at 10cm screed thickness); drywall-on-metal-frame walls → screed first, frame after (an uneven raw subfloor warps a metal track; volume-fill economics reverse for a stud-and-drywall assembly). Exception: a genuinely empty apartment with a concrete pump truck makes single-day full-contour screed the clear choice regardless of wall type.
- **"Continuous-pour screed is flatter" is significantly overstated**: real projects rarely achieve a genuinely continuous pour (hand-mixing constraints, room clutter) regardless of chosen method, and nearly all modern flooring needs a self-leveling correction layer anyway, which flattens ~3-8mm of variation regardless of how the screed was sequenced.
- **Template-stick leveling technique, genuinely new and broadly applicable**: mark a laser-level crossing point once on a wooden rod at the highest beacon, then match every other beacon to that single mark rather than repeatedly reading a tape measure — faster and more accurate (a tape blade flexes over distance; a rigid rod doesn't).
- **Pre-marking technique closing the walls-first accuracy gap**: before any walls exist, set a laser level once at the center and mark reference points at ~1m intervals across the entire future footprint (at 99cm above the corridor's finished floor — see the existing zero/working-reference content) — every future room then inherits an accurate mark from that single setup, avoiding the ~3-8mm room-to-room transfer error walls-first sequencing would otherwise accumulate.

### Furniture / Built-ins — Upper & Lower Kitchen Module Assembly, Additional Techniques (Category 4 batch, chunk 4, added 2026-08-19)

Full detail in [[03_Kitchen/Kitchen_Furniture|Kitchen Furniture]] (new sections). [sources: yt_19AUqOlliys, yt_-RsGqvH_hbo]

- **Upper-module-specific content, genuinely new for this store**: back-panel +4mm sizing rule; eurorail hanger install; a 3-pass alternating-direction range-hood duct-cutout technique specifically designed to avoid chip-out on a laminated panel face. [source: yt_19AUqOlliys]
- **Lower-module content, deliberately partial** (substantial overlap with the existing `yt_Y_Tuxcd-VxM` checklist not re-extracted): countertop-support bracket 50mm offset spec; inter-module tie (межсекционная стяжка) technique with exact drill coordinates; a bottle-rack module's deliberately-offset (not level) runner placement, a common hard-to-notice error if installed like a normal drawer; cross-module height alignment via laser level + adjustable legs, and cross-module depth alignment via a long straightedge. [source: yt_-RsGqvH_hbo]

### Kitchen — Never Place a Kitchen at a Window, 8 Reasons (Category 4 batch, chunk 4, added 2026-08-19)

Full detail in [[03_Kitchen/Kitchen_General|Kitchen: Layout & Configuration]] (new section). [source: yt_qzfABiffDB8]

- **8 mechanism-explained, client-testimonial-sourced reasons**: sink drainage routing conflict (sump-pump noise/reliability issues); cooktop/hood incompatibility (an opened window blows inward, reversing the intended venting effect); loss of upper-cabinet storage on an already-narrow run; fridge door-swing conflict forcing hinge reversal; a genuine physiological pupil-adaptation/glare injury risk (cuts, burns) from bright-to-dim transitions; window/blind access blocked by the countertop, forcing climbing; night task-lighting glare reflecting in a dark window; a radiator blocked by ~60cm of cabinetry unable to prevent window condensation, causing real countertop warping in a worked before/after case.

### Multi-Trade — On-Site QC Tour (Category 4 batch, chunk 5, added 2026-08-19)

Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_1_IcoSaNKP4_multitrade_qc_tour_049|extraction note]]. Content spans plumbing, windows, and heating — routed to each respective wiki page. [source: yt_1_IcoSaNKP4]

- **Never use polypropylene pipe — extends this store's existing PEX-over-polypropylene preference with a specific mechanism**: every weld/fusion joint measurably narrows the internal diameter, visible on a real cut-open joint (not on an along-the-pipe demo cut, which conveniently avoids showing this). Push-fit fitting technique: expand the pipe end twice (first pass sets a standard orientation, avoiding a misoriented second pass), insert immediately before the expanded end starts contracting. DIY tool rental: ~500-1,000 RUB/day, ~1,500-3,000 RUB total for a full apartment (2019, `unverified`).
- **Factory-assembled vs. site-assembled electrical panel, the real distinguishing factor**: not individual component quality — the assembled product's own warranty coverage and internal wiring/routing discipline, which only a factory-built, product-passport-certified unit provides. Never accept a site-assembled panel.
- **Window foam-joint width: 20-50mm, ~25mm optimum — routed to Windows_Installation_and_Fastening.md** — don't minimize purely to save foam or "clean up" the reveal; a narrower joint forces shorter, more-flexing fixing screws.
- **Radiator wall-embedding two-condition decision rule — routed to the Heating page**: only worth full embedding if both (1) the exterior wall is lightweight/aerated-concrete needing extra insulation there, and (2) an old air-heating void needs concealing; otherwise a shallow ~11-13mm recess suffices.
- **Never keep a developer-installed water-supply manifold** — cheapest-available by design (developer's incentive is minimum cost, not longevity), typically fails within ~6 months; always replace during rough works. Manifold-cabinet relocation into a shared building duct cavity (non-load-bearing, easily reopened/reclosed) is a legitimate accessibility technique.
- **Real family layout trade-off**: deliberately shrank an oversized bathroom and enlarged the adjoining toilet room to fit two separate sinks, specifically to avoid a morning-routine bottleneck for a family with multiple school-age children sharing one schedule.

### Kids Room — Sports Corner Minimum Configuration (Category 4 batch, chunk 5, added 2026-08-19)

Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_DZtR54pp68Q_kids_sports_corner_002|extraction note]]. Second Kids Room source in this store (below the 3+-source wiki-page threshold). [source: yt_DZtR54pp68Q]

- **A Swedish wall + pull-up bar alone is an inadequate default** — repeated client feedback: a child disengages within ~2 weeks regardless. **Minimum viable configuration**: inclined ladder, monkey-bars section, climbing rope, rings at varying heights, rope ladder (a cargo net is a bonus, not part of the minimum) — needs meaningfully more space than a single Swedish wall, so only viable in a sufficiently large apartment.
- **Removal-reversibility data point**: only 2 wall-mounting points (5 main + 1 auxiliary fixings), no floor fixing — full removal leaves only ~10 screw holes, easily patched. A useful figure for lowering the perceived commitment risk of built-in kids' gym equipment.

### Furniture / Built-ins — Upper Kitchen Module Mistakes, Second Source (Category 4 batch, chunk 5, added 2026-08-19)

Full detail in [[03_Kitchen/Kitchen_Furniture|Kitchen Furniture]] (new content added to the existing Upper Module Assembly section). [source: yt_Ljji4-Sxmzk]

- **10mm corrugated-duct oversizing rule** (complements, doesn't contradict, the rigid-duct cutting sequence already in this store from `yt_19AUqOlliys`); **back-panel staple/nail-only + ≤100mm perimeter spacing rule** (screws split the panel, forcing a multi-week replacement wait); **mounting-strip function explained** (ties side panels and locks the back panel — omitting it lets the back panel flex loose over time); **wire pass-through hole placement rule** (same vertical line as the sensor/switch, close to the module's bottom edge).

### Materials / Tools — Professional Crew Toolkits, Low Relevance (Category 4 batch, chunk 6, added 2026-08-19)

Two low-relevance sources, kept brief per this project's value-filter guidance. Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_bxy4oLXTcyI_installer_toolkit_055|note 1]] and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_5a59bBR480M_electrician_toolkit_046|note 2]]. [sources: yt_bxy4oLXTcyI, yt_5a59bBR480M]

- **Wall-chaser + vacuum interlock**: dust extraction switches on automatically the instant the chaser powers on — a checkable feature independent of brand, worth asking a hired electrician about. **Rotary-hammer safety clutch**: stops the tool within a fraction of a second when it binds, preventing the operator's arm/body from being spun — a real safety-relevant feature for overhead/through-wall drilling from a ladder. **Single-battery-platform economics**: standardizing a crew's cordless tools on one battery platform needs far fewer batteries/chargers than a naive per-tool count would suggest.

### Mistakes / Warnings — Designer-Project Defects, Mechanism-Explained (Category 5 trial batch, added 2026-08-19)

Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_Rlyx2F7Aaxg_designer_dunk_36lemons_315|extraction note]]. [source: yt_Rlyx2F7Aaxg]

- **WC/bathroom finished-floor level must exactly match the rest of the apartment — no threshold step.** A step too small to register as an obstacle (the brain reads it as flat floor) causes repeated, painful toe injuries, especially barefoot. `confirmed`, genuinely new safety mechanism.
- **Door swing-limiter (stopper) placement: 5-10cm from the door slab's edge on the handle side, never near the hinges.** A stopper near the hinge side sits on the short end of the door's own lever arm — the door's full swinging weight bends or tears it out on the first hard opens, leaving no functional stop.
- **Switch groups: horizontal only, exactly 90cm from the floor**, so any switch can be found by feel. A vertical group next to a normal horizontal switch on the same wall makes accidental simultaneous activation of the whole group much more likely.
- **Never use black finish plumbing fixtures** — a real observed pattern: visible residue streaks, smudges, and baked-on mineral deposits that can't be cleaned or touched up, appearing even on a fairly recent renovation; only fix is full replacement, and a black replacement shows the same defect again within months. `single-account`, genuinely new durability caution for this store.
- **Wardrobe/closet length should stop ~30cm short of its opening's full width** — at exactly 30cm short, a light switch fits centered in the gap between door casing and wardrobe edge once both are installed; shorter than that wastes usable storage for no benefit.
- **Only standard doors with an easy-to-clean finish are appropriate — never paint-grade/decorative-plaster doors**, since occupants grip and push the slab itself (not just the handle), producing permanent, unremovable smudge stains at the push point.
- **Laminate underlayment must be the manufacturer-specified product, never packaging offcuts** — a real defect placed cardboard offcuts as underlayment near floor-to-ceiling glazing prone to condensation, guaranteeing first-winter soak-through and rot.
- **Bathroom/WC primary lighting should be a backlit stretch-ceiling panel with side-glow, not directional spotlights** — soft even light for cleaning/flattering vs. harsh hot-spots and dim un-monitored floor areas.

### Planning Rules — Full-Apartment Replans, Two Sources (Category 5 trial batch, added 2026-08-19)

Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_hOdkXTI9k10_designer_dunk_975k_replan_273|note 1]] and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_JwB6L-tYd7I_designer_dunk_400k_replan_254|note 2]]. [sources: yt_hOdkXTI9k10, yt_JwB6L-tYd7I]

- **Wet-zone (bathroom/kitchen) placement should be driven by proximity to existing plumbing stacks, never by aesthetic preference (e.g. feng shui)** — a real defect: a designer placed a "master" bathroom by window-orientation reasoning with no stack nearby, forcing a 25cm screed raise and a loud sump pump next to the bedroom — the client's decisive reason for firing the designer. [source: yt_hOdkXTI9k10]
- **"Wardrobe-split-into-two/three" storage technique**: an S-curved partition splits one storage volume into 2-3 separately-accessed wardrobes (e.g. hallway + bedroom access to the same footprint) — has a practical subdivision limit (a real "seven-wardrobe" over-application produced individually near-useless nooks). [source: yt_JwB6L-tYd7I]
- **Sofa-to-large-format-TV minimum distance: 2.7m**, to avoid the screen feeling "in your face." `confirmed`, explicit numeric target. [source: yt_JwB6L-tYd7I]
- **Fold-away-bed-into-storage-wall technique** can convert a small guest room into flexible-use space (home gym/yoga) on demand — a real client wishlist item assumed impossible to fit was solved this way. [source: yt_JwB6L-tYd7I]
- **Exterior corners should be routed away from both impact paths (foot traffic) and wear paths (door-swing/drawer-face rubbing) — two distinct failure mechanisms**, not one generic "avoid corners" rule. [source: yt_JwB6L-tYd7I]
- **A full technical-brief checklist is a reusable template structure**: lock wet zones to stacks first, then fixed-requirement rooms, then flexible-use spaces last — matches this store's existing hard-vs-flexible-dimension framework, demonstrated here at full-apartment scale. [source: yt_hOdkXTI9k10]

### Mistakes / Warnings — Fraudulent "Move-In-Ready" Renovation, Full QC Walkthrough (Category 5 trial batch, added 2026-08-19)

Densest source in this trial, ~25 QC/fraud points. Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_o7lGqaUuGm0_fake_renovation_scam_walkthrough_214|extraction note]]. [source: yt_o7lGqaUuGm0]

- **⚠️ Fabricated underfloor heating — a genuine consumer-fraud finding, routed to [[12_Engineering_and_Systems/Heating|Heating]]'s new due-diligence section.** Visible, apparently-working thermostats are not evidence of a working system underneath — verify independently (cable detector, or confirm actual floor warming) before valuing a "pre-installed" heating claim.
- **A water heater or similarly serviceable appliance must be planned with real future access** — a real defect: mounted first, then permanently walled/tiled around, requiring full wall demolition to repair or replace once it failed.
- **Toilet seat height: 42cm from floor; bowl rim without seat: 40cm (400mm)** — independently corroborates this store's existing 40cm wall-hung-toilet installation figure (`yt_WjbLMKON_s8`), routed to `08_WC/WC_Guide.md`.
- **AC pre-wiring claims must be verified for completeness, not just cable presence** — a real defect: only a 220V cable existed (spliced onto a general-purpose circuit), no refrigerant-line conduits, no dedicated breaker — "ready for AC" was false.
- **Window trim secondary-plastic yellowing, extending this store's existing sill-end-cap caution** — routed to `13_Surfaces_and_Finishes/analysis/Windows_Slope_Finishing.md`.
- **A "ready to move in" renovation claim should be independently verified room by room before treating it as sunk value** — the source's own closing framing, after a real 4.5M RUB net loss from trusting a pre-renovated unit's claimed condition.

### Furniture / Built-ins — Closet Niche Beside a Door, Load-Bearing-Wall Junction (Remainder-pool batch, added 2026-08-19)

New-build layout critique (#116). Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_o4KitYl8vpU_closet_niche_next_to_load_bearing_wall_116|extraction note]]. [source: yt_o4KitYl8vpU]

- **⚠️ When building a new partition off a load-bearing wall to fit a built-in closet beside a bedroom door in one short run**: closet niche minimum **700mm** wide (600mm-deep closet + returns); door-opening niche minimum **900mm** wide (800mm minimum habitable-room door + ~100mm returns each side). Combined run from the load-bearing wall: **minimum 1.60m, ideally 1.70m** — undersizing risks an undersized door, a bad closet/frame junction, or a closet shallower than 500mm. Distinct from (and narrower/more specific than) this store's existing free-standing walk-in-closet sizing formulas (`yt_RyHzFDGgqKA`) — cross-reference, don't merge.

### Walls / Ceilings — Wall-Plane Flush Alignment Technique (Remainder-pool batch, added 2026-08-19)

Technical-design mini-masterclass (#113). Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_Af8nNyn9a_c_flush_wall_plane_technical_design_113|extraction note]]. [source: yt_Af8nNyn9a_c]

- **⚠️ A short wall segment out of plane with an adjacent load-bearing wall (an external+internal corner "step") should almost always be rebuilt flush, not left as-is** — the resulting pocket is too shallow to furnish, only collects dirt, and costs more in finishing labor/materials (extra corner taping, wallpaper/paint work, baseboard routing, stretch-ceiling perimeter routing) than fixing it at rough-in. If the segment is itself load-bearing (can't move), fur it out flush or skim/level it directly — either can shift a nearby door opening by a small offset (~7cm in the documented example) to hide the correction.
- **Quantified exception: in ~10% of cases, deliberately move the segment outward instead** — only when doing so creates a genuinely useful storage niche, not a dead unfurnishable gap. Default remains flush-align in the other ~90%.

### Kitchen / Loggia / Electrical / Plumbing — Combined Kitchen-Living-Loggia Numeric Planning Sequence (Remainder-pool batch, added 2026-08-19)

Dense numeric technical-design walkthrough on a 79.5 m² free-plan new-build (#154). Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_WmkOC9uKnCQ_kitchen_living_loggia_layout_lifehack_154|extraction note]]. [source: yt_WmkOC9uKnCQ]

- **Structural pre-demo survey practice**: physically open shafts/ducts/boxes (hammer/rotary hammer) during measurement to confirm what can be removed, rather than trusting drawings alone.
- **Partition wall between a kitchen-living room and adjacent rooms: minimum 150mm thick** for soundproofing — a further (7th) independent corroboration of this store's well-established 150mm-partition figure, this time from a planning-technique video rather than a defect-critique video.
- **Door/opening in a perpendicular wall segment: 900mm opening width, 100mm returns each side** (≥1100mm total run needed).
- **Kitchen cabinet run depth: 600mm.**
- **⚠️ Plumbing rough-in coordinate sequence for a kitchen sink + dishwasher wall (reusable ordering rule)**: hot water for the sink → cold water for the sink (to its right) → cold drinking water (below that) → cold water for the dishwasher (below that). Two drains on the same wall: one sink, one dishwasher. Genuinely new sequencing rule, not previously recorded in this store's plumbing rough-in content.
- **Radiator niche sizing formula**: from finished floor, minimum 850mm clearance before the niche; niche begins 100mm down from its own top edge; niche dimensions = grille's **rear-seat (mounting) dimensions + 5mm**, explicitly **not** the grille's visible/decorative face dimensions. Worked example: 1000×600mm grille → 1005×605mm niche. **⚠️ Same-project overlap**: this figure (and the "+800mm opening width" figure) is near-identical to the existing `yt_CHCB4KPupyc` entry (#156, same channel, same 79.5m² apartment) in `14_Furniture/analysis/Wardrobe_Worked_Cases.md` — almost certainly the same real project shown across two videos, not an independent second data point. Recorded as corroboration, not new evidence.
- **Balcony-door-opening design rule**: never shrink a sofa's fixed clearance zone (back+seat depth) to enlarge a window/light opening — forces the sofa into the door's swing path or blocks the door leaf's own single-direction opening.
- **Loggia zoning fix (general pattern)**: relocate the loggia entry point so storage is reachable from both sides rather than requiring a full-length walk (or a crawl under drying laundry) — documented result: 0 → 2 usable storage zones on the same loggia footprint.
- Loggia lighting centered on the balcony block; switch group clustered near the sofa controlling two circuits (kitchen + loggia); minimum two sockets recommended on the balcony door slope.

### Planning Rules — Full Replan, 111.5m² New-Build (Remainder-pool batch, added 2026-08-19)

Real full design+build project, dense with techniques. **Confirmed title/content mismatch** — "worst apartment of 2019" title, actually a positive full-replan case study; also disconfirms the working hypothesis that #5xx-numbered videos are systematically off-topic (this one, #520, is dense and on-topic; only `LVerbq1hkxg` #549 was a one-off exception). Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_v7UXJ5fJ0H0_worst_apartment_2019_replan_520|extraction note]]. [source: yt_v7UXJ5fJ0H0]

- **⚠️ A developer-built wall at 135° instead of 90° between a kitchen and an adjacent corridor cost the kitchen ~1.10m of usable width** — rebuilt to true 90°, recovering the width without shrinking any other room. General principle: verify every wall's actual angle before accepting a developer layout as fixed; a small angular deviation compounds into a large loss over a wall's length, and left uncorrected causes cabinetry-installation seam misalignment.
- **⚠️ A door-opening-swing diagnostic**: if a door opening's position (e.g. on a diagonal corner) means the door leaf conflicts with something in every possible swing direction, the fix is almost always relocating the opening itself, not compromising on a "least-bad" swing.
- **A shower stall's clear space is highly sensitive to small dimensional changes** — a real case where moving one wall just 10cm was the specific difference between "usable, if snug" and "too tight to use without hitting fixtures with your elbows." Treat 10cm increments near a shower-clearance minimum as consequential, not negligible.
- **Loggia insulation-removal-for-area-gain technique**: verify a loggia's actual wall composition before joining it; removing an unnecessary existing insulation layer once properly re-glazed/warmed can itself recover usable floor area (documented: ~10cm per side, ~1 m² total gained) in addition to replacing cold/wind-only glazing with warm glazing.
- **Wardrobe wall-shift technique**: an L-shaped storage run that doesn't fit its allotted space can sometimes be fixed by shifting the bounding wall outward without the corridor-width tradeoff it appears to cost — documented case: 20cm wall shift fully fixed the wardrobe while leaving the corridor at a still-generous 1.40m.
- **⚠️ Entrance-vestibule reclamation technique, with a hard safety caveat**: a small mudroom/vestibule area can sometimes be reclaimed from shared building-corridor/stairwell-landing space by moving the true entry door outward — described as "always worth it, even at some risk of being required to reverse it." **Critical, non-negotiable caveat**: never let this kind of reclamation block or obstruct anything that must remain accessible to any person (emergency personnel, maintenance, communal utility access) when the occupant isn't home.

### Project Management — Client Workflow & Process, Turnkey Interview (Remainder-pool batch, added 2026-08-19)

Client Q&A interview about turnkey project process. Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_f1KpZOQSZgQ_client_workflow_interview_60m2_513|extraction note]]. [source: yt_f1KpZOQSZgQ]

- **Single-point-of-contact team structure**: on a project involving up to ~14 different tradespeople, the client communicates with exactly one foreman/project manager who coordinates every trade and all utility/management-company contact — a durable evaluation question for any turnkey provider ("is there a true single point of contact through construction, not just sales").
- **Materials-procurement split, transferable to self-managed**: rough materials fully company-handled; finish materials client-selected (~90-100% online) but company-ordered/picked-up/inspected/paid, with receipts retained specifically for warranty claims — a clean itemized-accountability practice worth replicating even outside a turnkey arrangement.
- **Client time-commitment pattern across a project's lifecycle**: near-weekly visits in the first ~2 months (curiosity-driven), tapering to minimal once rough works are trusted, rising again during finish-material selection — useful expectation-setting for a self-managed owner's own time planning.
- **⚠️ Design caution: a decorative kitchen-countertop "bump-out" step with no functional storage use is a real trip/collision hazard** between adjacent work zones — round the edges if pursuing it purely for aesthetics, or skip it.
- **⚠️ Design caution: printed/patterned large-format porcelain tile (imitation stone/marble) can show visible print-quality inconsistency across pieces** (uneven tinting, some cut pieces missing the print entirely) — keep tile selection simple in a room that already has other visual-interest elements, reducing exposure to this defect risk.

### Mistakes / Warnings — Construction-Middleman Scam Mechanism (Remainder-pool batch, added 2026-08-19, PARTIAL — named-individual content excluded)

Consumer-protection exposé; the specific accused individual's identity and the video's personal/vindictive framing were deliberately excluded per this project's standing named-individual-dispute convention — only the generalizable scam mechanism and red flags were extracted. Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_ZvyXuUJ__Ag_middleman_scam_mechanism_511|extraction note]]. [source: yt_ZvyXuUJ__Ag]

- **⚠️ Documented middleman-markup mechanism**: an unlicensed operator prices work by calling ~5 real tradespeople for quotes, always uses the *highest* quote as baseline cost, pads 10% for missed items, then adds an operator markup (25-30% starting, 40-50% once established) — or, in a more predatory variant, takes a full 50% cut of *total client payment* while still requiring subcontracted tradespeople to supply their own tools. Ask any general contractor directly what their markup structure is over subcontracted labor.
- **⚠️ Red flags for vetting any contractor**: a large upfront deposit (documented pattern: 40-50% of total project value) relative to verifiable work-in-progress; an office that turns out to be borrowed/rented by the hour rather than genuinely occupied; crew recruited anonymously through classifieds/employment-center placements via a separate "work phone," keeping true business structure opaque. Cross-references this store's existing anti-upsell/smeta-literacy heuristics (`yt_cdNwbqsLUK4`).

### Project Management — Expense-Tracking Methodology, 5 Methods Ranked (Remainder-pool batch, added 2026-08-19)

New topic area for this store — a genuinely new, high-relevance-to-self-managed-projects methodology source. Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_3PJJTDkppUg_expense_tracking_methodology_504|extraction note]]. [source: yt_3PJJTDkppUg]

- **Five renovation expense-tracking methods, ranked worst to best**: (1) do nothing, reconstruct from memory at the end — worst, loses most information; (2) collect all receipts, tally at the end — misses every cash/no-receipt payment (informal labor, movers, market deliveries) and can't answer "how much so far" mid-project; (3) ⚠️ delegate tracking to the crew/contractor — riskiest, direct incentive to pad receipts and pocket the difference, and degrades back into method 2 at reporting time; (4) ⚠️ fixed-price rough materials from the contractor, track only finish materials yourself — creates a direct incentive for the contractor to buy cheaper materials and pocket the difference; only viable with a highly trusted company backing a long warranty; (5) ⚠️ a dedicated spreadsheet system — the recommended method, directly applicable to a self-managed project.
- **Spreadsheet structure (method 5), fully detailed**: a Summary sheet aggregating 4 blocks (Primary Expenses — rough/finish materials, electrical, plumbing, windows/balconies, doors; Secondary Expenses — AC units, kitchen, furniture, appliances, lighting, accessories, deferrable up to a year post-handover; Works — contracted total, paid-to-date, remaining; Balance — total handed to the crew, spent, cash remaining in hand); per-category purchase sheets (date/item/qty/unit price, auto-summed); a dedicated Payments sheet with **every payment tied to a memorable event** (a real dispute-prevention practice); a dedicated Overrun sheet isolating planned-vs-actual price deltas for any substitution decision (e.g. laminate → parquet).
- **Usability features worth replicating in any DIY tracking spreadsheet**: auto-highlighting delivery/loading line items; a frozen header row; a "scroll-back-up" visual cue cell; protected/locked formula cells (data-entry mistakes can only happen in data cells); pinned per-sheet subtotals near the top; cross-sheet hyperlinks.

### Planning Rules — Full Replan, New-Build (#252, Remainder-pool batch, added 2026-08-19)

Dense full-replan case with several genuinely new findings and a valuable nuance to the store's most-corroborated figure. Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_n7X10oIqugU_full_replan_252|extraction note]]. [source: yt_n7X10oIqugU]

- **A closet/storage niche can be built too deep, not just too shallow** — a 1.10m-deep niche when a hanging-storage zone only needs 600mm wastes the remaining ~50cm; complements this store's existing too-shallow-niche corroboration chain with the opposite failure mode.
- **⚠️ Always positively identify what an exposed/protruding pipe or riser actually does before finalizing a design around it** — a real case had a completely unidentified pipe (even the developer couldn't explain it) and a separate riser missing its cold-water leg entirely.
- **⚠️ Nuance to the well-established 150mm soundproofing-partition figure**: when a full-depth closet run is built directly against a partition for its whole length, the closet itself already exceeds a bare 150mm wall's sound isolation — the partition can drop to 100mm without a net soundproofing loss in that specific configuration, freeing 50mm for a wider closet or a shifted door. Qualifies, doesn't contradict, the 150mm default.
- **⚠️ Meta-caution**: a stated design rationale in a walkthrough is not automatically the true constraint behind a dimension — the source demonstrates this on its own content (a "pipe clearance" explanation for a number that was actually driven by an unrelated furniture decision). Treat narrated justification with some skepticism.
- **WC minimum comfortable width: 1.10m** — **⚠️ corrected on cross-check**: this is not an unlinked figure — it's an independent match for `08_WC/WC_Guide.md`'s existing "comfortable width with a 25cm sink: 1.10m" formula (§1), now corroborated by a second source (see that page's own update). Wall-alignment-at-a-break technique (build both segments of a shaft-interrupted wall run flush to one shared reference plane). Home-network/server hub placement idea: dedicate a wardrobe's overhead tier to outlets/cable pass-throughs/equipment storage.

### Doors/Trim / Walls-Ceilings / Kitchen — Full Replan, Detailed Numeric Planning-Logic Masterclass (#234, Remainder-pool batch, added 2026-08-19)

One of the most methodical numeric-planning sources in this remainder pool, including a demonstrated design self-review practice. Full detail in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_8Xy-h8cS_-s_full_replan_worst_two_room_234|extraction note]]. [source: yt_8Xy-h8cS_-s]

- **⚠️ Asymmetric tile+adhesive compensation technique**: compensate a raw pre-finish opening's width **per bounding wall's own flatness**, not with a flat blanket figure — a freshly-rebuilt flat wall needs ~15mm, an existing crooked surface that can't be demolished (e.g. a vent-shaft face) needs closer to 25mm. Worked example: net 1000mm target → 1040mm raw dimension (15+25), not a naive 1030mm (15+15).
- **⚠️ Default partition-thickness matrix with an infrastructure-priority override**: 150mm between habitable rooms, 100mm elsewhere by default — but even a storage-dividing wall stays at 150mm if it must house mandatory infrastructure (water manifold, electrical panel), overriding the "obvious" thinner-wall-for-more-storage instinct.
- **⚠️ Door-opening width is fixed and non-negotiable; reveal/return width is flexible and just absorbs the remainder, with a 100mm floor** — a return under 100mm forces the casing to be notched/trimmed.
- **⚠️ Counterintuitive passage-opening-width rule**: a wider passage opening between a kitchen-living room and a hallway doesn't come "for free" — opening and reveal width both subtract directly from the adjoining room's own floor area. Keep passage openings at their functional minimum rather than a feel-good wider default.
- **Kitchen-module cost-neutral filler-strip technique**: keep cabinet modules at a standard 600mm width and absorb any layout-run leftover in free-width filler strips instead — filler-strip width doesn't affect price, non-standard module width does.
- **Closet-behind-a-door depth-maximizing technique**: push the door opening to one extreme edge of a wall run (not centered) so the entire remainder goes to an adjacent closet's depth.
- **⚠️ Deliberate top-down design self-review practice**: after a first-draft layout looks "done," do one dedicated top-down review pass specifically hunting for cross-zone furniture conflicts and secondary storage gaps invisible while designing each zone individually — a real demonstrated catch (a wardrobe/nightstand door conflict, a missing quick-access coat-storage zone) led to a full zone redesign.
- Bedroom acoustic layer against a neighbor-shared wall: 50mm (distinct from the 150mm interior-partition default — this is an added layer on an exterior/party wall). Sink-to-future-tiled-wall clearance: minimum 15mm. Washing-machine/dryer-in-hallway-closet acoustic-comfort choice flagged `uncertain` on code compliance (source is evasive when asked directly).

### Planning Rules — Narrated-Planning-Logic Cluster, Final 5 Videos (Remainder-pool batch, closes out the original 17-video list, added 2026-08-19)

Five more videos from the same July-November 2023 narrated-planning-logic production cluster already contributing `8Xy-h8cS_-s`/`n7X10oIqugU` above (`ZVoExA0t6nI` #223, `1pU60p0Jh3A` #228, `HVSZh0lH9hk` #230, `W9cCxVQVVes` #232, `D8t1ADisUE8` #246). **Heavy corroboration across all five** (150mm/100mm partition defaults with the infrastructure-priority override, entrance-door 1000mm→900mm downsizing — now corroborated 5+ times within this cluster alone, plan color-coding for masonry-vs-drywall, 15mm tile-buildup compensation, radiator-niche sizing, 900mm-opening/100mm-return formula) — only genuinely new content extracted below. **Title-similarity duplicate-risk check resolved**: `1pU60p0Jh3A` (flagged against `8Xy-h8cS_-s` in the tracking file) is confirmed an independent project, not a reused script. Full detail in each video's own extraction note: [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_ZVoExA0t6nI_useless_3bedroom_223|ZVoExA0t6nI]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_1pU60p0Jh3A_worst_two_room_228|1pU60p0Jh3A]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_HVSZh0lH9hk_craziest_two_room_230|HVSZh0lH9hk]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_W9cCxVQVVes_hardest_one_room_232|W9cCxVQVVes]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_D8t1ADisUE8_odnushka_premium_replan_246|D8t1ADisUE8]].

- **⚠️⚠️ Major risk-disclosure technique**: relocating a bedroom to sit directly below an upstairs neighbor's *kitchen* (a wet zone, not just a bathroom) carries a real water-damage risk — only do this on the top floor (no neighbor above) or after obtaining a confirmed guarantee that the upstairs neighbor has completed full waterproofing on their own kitchen. [source: D8t1ADisUE8]
- **⚠️ Wall-squareness DIY diagnostic**: place any true rectangular reference object into a suspect corner and rotate it flush against one wall — the gap/overlap at the other edge visually reveals how far the corner deviates from 90°, no instruments needed. [source: HVSZh0lH9hk]
- **⚠️ Curved/rounded wall corners are a fully mechanism-explained developer defect**: costlier plastering, persistent waviness/wallpaper bubbling, a curved curtain track that reliably jams, and a straight-rod "fix" that wastes floor area. **Convert any curved wall segment into two straight, faceted angled segments instead.** [source: ZVoExA0t6nI]
- **⚠️ Pre-purchase inspection checklist item**: verify every intended room's doorway geometry can fit at least a 700mm-leaf door before buying — a real case (91cm raw corner-to-column gap) forced a 55mm-per-side reveal, leaving only a "most shameful" 600mm door or abandoning the room as separate. [source: 1pU60p0Jh3A]
- **⚠️ Wall thickness beyond 100mm brings no soundproofing benefit once an interior door is present in that wall** — the door leaf itself is the binding acoustic constraint, not the wall. Distinct from the 150mm-partition default, which applies to solid partition runs. [source: 1pU60p0Jh3A]
- **⚠️ Wet-exit-distance door-placement rule**: place a bathroom/shower-room door opening on the side nearer the towel warmer, not the side that looks more convenient relative to the sink, so a wet person doesn't have to cross the whole room. **Explicit storage-vs-hatch priority order**: when a storage cabinet's minimum depth (400mm) conflicts with an access hatch, shrink the hatch, never the storage. [source: HVSZh0lH9hk]
- **Washing-machine vibration-clearance mechanism**: use 700mm clearance (not 600mm) near a washing machine — it vibrates in operation and will bang against an adjacent sink/wall if too tight; add a full 5cm buffer beyond nominal appliance width for this reason specifically. [sources: HVSZh0lH9hk (50mm behind-appliance service clearance for water/electrical), D8t1ADisUE8]
- **Bidirectional (double-sided) radiator technique**: remove a partition segment around a radiator positioned in a dividing wall and add grilles on both faces — one radiator heats two adjacent zones, useful for panoramic-glazing condensation control when in-floor convectors aren't an option. [source: D8t1ADisUE8]
- **Neighbor-coordinated acoustic-layer technique**: when an adjacent unit's owner is also a client, coordinate sound-insulation layers on both sides of the shared wall for a combined result neither side could achieve alone. [source: D8t1ADisUE8]
- **Segment-tier-dependent sizing/design exceptions**: several of this channel's "default" figures are premium-tier upsizes, not universal minimums — a toilet/hygienic-shower zone at 1000mm instead of the usual 800mm minimum; centered (away-from-walls) furniture placement as a deliberate premium design choice where the practitioner's usual default is to avoid it. **Practical distinction**: an entrance door that can't be replaced (HOA/developer-locked) can often still have its *opening position* adjusted even though the fixture itself is fixed. [source: D8t1ADisUE8]
- **Plan-documentation color-coding convention**: draw new drywall-on-frame construction in a different color/fill than original masonry on any project plan, so a crew/inspector/client can immediately distinguish structure from added framing — corroborated 4 times across this cluster. [sources: W9cCxVQVVes, 1pU60p0Jh3A, and others in this cluster]
- **Hygiene-driven path-elongation technique**: deliberately route the entrance-to-kitchen walking path *longer* than the shortest available option, to put more distance between the dirty-shoe zone and food-prep areas. [source: HVSZh0lH9hk]
- **Kitchen module-width discipline**: never widen a cabinet module beyond an adjacent fixed-width appliance's own width (e.g. keep a storage module at 60cm next to a 60cm fridge) — an oversized module next to a fixed appliance reads as visually disproportionate. **Minimum comfortable linear-kitchen width: ~2.30m** below which no layout reconfiguration fixes the cramped result. [sources: HVSZh0lH9hk, ZVoExA0t6nI]

### Remainder-Pool Round 2 — First Batch (4 videos, added 2026-08-19)

**New candidate pool re-derived** by cross-referencing the full `_Inbox/transcripts/channel_fresh_titles.txt` channel manifest (158 rows) against every `watch?v=` URL in `00_Master/processed_sources.csv` — found 16 IDs never covered by any prior triage pass (Category 4, Category 5, or the Round 1 remainder-pool list). See `_Inbox/planning/zemskov_remainder_pool_20260819.md`'s "Round 2" table for the full list. This first batch of 4 (`DEs8V-mHHDo` #169, `C4lUAfJyyb0` #170, `Qw2Xi6uPLls` #302, `MWDcYHqe-iQ` #288) all fetched cleanly and cleared the value-filter bar. Full detail in each source's own extraction note: [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_DEs8V-mHHDo_kitchen_7_mistakes_169|DEs8V-mHHDo]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_C4lUAfJyyb0_developer_stolen_meters_170|C4lUAfJyyb0]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_Qw2Xi6uPLls_floating_dimension_drafting_technique_302|Qw2Xi6uPLls]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_MWDcYHqe-iQ_designer_apartment_walkthrough_288|MWDcYHqe-iQ]].

- **⚠️⚠️ Standout finding — the "floating dimension" construction-drafting technique**: on any construction drawing with a chain of dimensioned segments, specify only the dimensions that are genuinely functionally critical, and deliberately leave one non-critical segment's width unlabeled. A crew builds outward from the specified anchor points; since cumulative construction/plastering tolerance error has nowhere labeled to go, it automatically lands in the unlabeled ("floating") segment instead of eating into a dimension that must stay firm (worked example: a wardrobe's depth held to a firm 600mm by specifying only "600mm offset from a corner" and "150mm wall thickness" on one sheet, plus "500mm reveal" and "800mm opening" on a separate openings sheet — leaving one reveal deliberately unlabeled). **A general, broadly reusable construction-documentation principle**, not specific to any one room. [source: Qw2Xi6uPLls]
- **⚠️ Electrical-panel recess sizing formula**: minimum 110mm panel installation depth → build the niche to at least 140mm deep; if the host wall isn't thick enough to net a sound remainder after recessing, thicken it first (worked example: 200mm → 250mm). Full coordinate sequence: 1200mm up from the floor screed for the niche bottom, +500mm for niche height; 100mm in from the neighbor wall, +400mm for niche width. [source: C4lUAfJyyb0]
- **⚠️ Sequencing rule: model a wall's soundproofing-layer thickness before positioning any door opening near that wall** — getting this backwards forces the door casing to be notched/trimmed later to fit around the added layer, which looks worse than an intentionally asymmetric opening reveal chosen from the start (worked example: 115mm/185mm asymmetric reveal, driven by a 70mm acoustic layer — a second data point for this layer thickness, distinct from the more commonly-seen 50mm figure). [source: C4lUAfJyyb0]
- **Kitchen QC checklist, 7 points**: 15-20cm curtain-clearance gap between a kitchen run's end and a side wall; exactly 600mm countertop-to-upper-cabinet clearance for a clean 60cm-tile backsplash fit (with a fix technique if built wrong); a light-control switch must be reachable from within the actual cook zone, not tucked in a hard-to-reach corner; verify appliance door-swing/control-panel side before corner placement; floor-to-ceiling upper cabinets cost up to 1.5× more and the top tier needs a step stool regardless of height; a deep storage column needs a pull-out drawer system, not a simple hinged door, to use its full depth; extend upper-cabinet depth to a full 600mm around a corner-adjacent fridge rather than leaving a shallow, dust-collecting shelf above it. [source: DEs8V-mHHDo]
- **⚠️ "Appliance parking zone" (паркинг техники) clarified**: it's an extension of the main countertop at the *same height*, for appliances used *infrequently* — not any recessed/sunken nook. A documented misuse (a sunken nook mislabeled as a parking zone, functioning as neither a real parking zone nor a usable prep surface, plus creating an unnecessary sharp corner) sharpens this store's existing concept. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **⚠️ Kitchen cook-zone continuity rule**: when a fixed obstruction (e.g. a vent shaft) sits within a kitchen run, push the whole run outward past it rather than splitting the cook zone into two disconnected halves around it — splitting doesn't gain space, it breaks usability and creates an unnecessary sharp corner. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **⚠️ Door casing/baseboard color-and-material-consistency rule**: casings and baseboards should be the same color, ideally the same manufacturer/material, reading as one continuous trim element — never try to paint a door to match an adjacent wood-textured surface's exact color (use contrast instead). **Flooring-to-door-frame transition**: meet the frame directly with a small color-matched-sealant gap, never a separate visible threshold strip with exposed fasteners. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **⚠️ Master-switch differentiation rule**: a "master" switch that cuts power to the whole unit must sit at a different height/position and look visually distinct from the regular switch group, to prevent accidental presses. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **⚠️ Baseboard-integrated electrical distribution caution**: routing outlets/TV coax/network cabling through a baseboard channel system (rather than fixed wall points at furniture height) creates a furniture-placement conflict — plan electrical/data points at their intended furniture-height locations instead. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **⚠️ Combined wall-thickening technique**: thicken a wall by ~10cm to shift a bathtub's outer edge forward, simultaneously eliminating an awkward hard-to-clean ledge next to the tub *and* improving a wall-mounted mixer's reach from within the tub. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **Bedroom door-reveal correction: 10cm, not 30cm**, on the side pushed toward for maximum adjacent-closet depth. **Decorative backlighting over a headboard**: striking for rental-listing photos, genuinely uncomfortable for daily use — independently corroborates the segment-tier-dependent design-choice theme first seen in `D8t1ADisUE8`. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]

### Remainder-Pool Round 2 — Second Batch (4 videos, added 2026-08-19)

Continuing Round 2's 16-video re-derived candidate list. Full detail in each source's own extraction note: [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_8mffcO5q-k_microwave_coffee_machine_slot_191|_8mffcO5q-k]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_OnKreLhmLYY_90_mistakes_part3_299|OnKreLhmLYY]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_nbrDFET2AXk_90_mistakes_part2_295|nbrDFET2AXk]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_vRLdOS5HTTU_got_back_stolen_space_298|vRLdOS5HTTU]]. **⚠️ Same-project note**: `OnKreLhmLYY` (#299) and `nbrDFET2AXk` (#295) are parts 3 and 2 of the same "90 mistakes" critique series about one single apartment — their overlapping findings (extreme switch count, a drywall-ceiling chemical-exposure claim) are the same practitioner re-narrating the same real defects, not independent corroboration; flagged in both source notes rather than double-counted here.

- **⚠️ Appliance tower-slot assignment should be driven by usage *frequency* and *maintenance/refill* action, not just each appliance's own "ideal ergonomic height" in isolation** — real client self-correction case: a coffee machine used multiple times daily was moved to the easily-reached lower tower slot (swapping with a rarely-used microwave), because the upper slot's bean-hopper refill point sat at ~1.75m, requiring an unreachable ~60cm-above-the-machine reach for a task performed several times a day. A household with staff to handle refills wouldn't hit this problem — the client's own hands-on refill routine is what surfaced it. [source: _8mffcO5q-k]
- **⚠️⚠️ Major finding: "improved"/denser door weatherstripping can break a magnetic door lock, and doesn't actually improve soundproofing anyway** — the door bounces off a denser seal before the magnetic latch engages, and the real sound-leak path (the gap under the door) isn't addressed by a denser perimeter seal at all. Don't pair a magnetic lock with "upgraded" weatherstripping without verifying compatibility. [source: OnKreLhmLYY]
- **⚠️ Walk-in-closet-style open storage vs. simple wardrobe: a real case quantifies ~2.5× more floor area consumed for the *same* clothing capacity**, plus an exposed unprotected external corner — a concrete data point for this store's existing wardrobe-vs-walk-in tradeoff content (`14_Furniture/analysis/Wardrobe_vs_Walkin_Tradeoff.md`). [source: OnKreLhmLYY]
- **⚠️ Textured wall finish + raking-light fixture interaction**: a light fixture that casts side/raking light visually amplifies every irregularity in a textured wall finish, reading as poor workmanship even when the texture is intentional — pair texture with diffuse-light fixtures instead. **Diagonal tile-transition** needs a real justification — without one it's just extra cuts/waste. **Glossy windowsill in a kitchen**: fingerprints, installation imperfections, and appliance scratches (especially if outlets in the reveal invite appliance placement there) all show more on glossy than matte, and a matte-frame pairing looks worse than either alone. [source: OnKreLhmLYY]
- **⚠️ "Butt-first design" anti-pattern, named explicitly**: building a symmetric/decorative wall composition *before* confirming where functionally-necessary furniture will go — once the furniture lands where actually needed, it can wreck the intended symmetry. Confirm functional furniture placement first, decorative compositions second, never the reverse. [source: OnKreLhmLYY]
- **⚠️ Grid/lattice ceiling light fixture over a bed: avoid** — the grid blocks most of the light output *and* accumulates dust that continuously sheds onto the person below. **Bathtub-then-tile sequencing, stated definitively**: install the tub first flush against three walls, then tile *over* its rim — never tile first and push the tub against a finished perimeter. [source: nbrDFET2AXk]
- **⚠️ Two-piece/nested decorative door handles develop noticeable play within ~1 week, worsening to real looseness by ~6 months** — noisy, uncomfortable, and the resulting mechanical edge can injure. Avoid decorative multi-piece handle assemblies for durability. [source: nbrDFET2AXk]
- **⚠️ Duct-box/niche "fill vs. finish" decision heuristic**: a developer niche that isn't functionally needed is usually cheaper and more useful filled flat with lightweight masonry (~10cm aerated block) than finished as a feature — a flat filled wall also lets kitchen-module electrical points land without chasing an adjacent load-bearing wall. [source: nbrDFET2AXk]
- **⚠️ Rendering skepticism, stated as general buyer guidance**: designers have an incentive to show "interesting geometry" in a rendering to justify their fee — complex features that look striking rendered often look worse built and cost significantly more; ask what a feature will actually look/cost like built, not just how it renders. [source: nbrDFET2AXk]
- **⚠️ Wardrobe/closet-niche dimensional-consistency check**: verify a storage niche's width is uniform top-to-middle-to-bottom *before* choosing a door mechanism — a real ~4cm inconsistency made neither a sliding nor hinged system installable, forcing a downgrade to a shower-curtain-on-a-wire improvisation. [source: nbrDFET2AXk]
- **⚠️ Remote-client digitization workflow**: an accurate 3D model of an existing space can be built without an in-person site visit — a digitization specialist collects measurements remotely by phone/call, a project lead separately interviews the client to build the technical brief, then a technical department reviews/coordinates. A reusable process model for a design engagement that can't start with a site visit. **"Conflict color" plan-marking technique**: mark any wish-list item that doesn't fully fit a real layout in a distinct color during planning, rather than silently accepting a partial fit. [source: vRLdOS5HTTU]
- **Loggia rest-zone minimum width: ~1.10m, ideally 1.20m**, after glazing/insulation buildup — distinct from this store's general loggia-width content, specifically for a seating/lounging use. A second data point for the loggia-insulation-removal-for-space-gain technique: 17cm of width recovered in this project (vs. ~10cm/side, ~1m² total in an earlier source). [source: vRLdOS5HTTU]

### Remainder-Pool Round 2 — Third Batch (2 extracted + 1 duplicate-skip + 1 partial, added 2026-08-19)

Continuing Round 2's re-derived candidate list. Full detail in each source's own extraction note: [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_pNhM-kKBy6A_knowledge_base_reveal_294|pNhM-kKBy6A]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_u3UuZN9LHg0_developer_gandon_irregular_geometry_268|u3UuZN9LHg0]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_eezwcNG-1qI_designer_project_walkthrough_269|eezwcNG-1qI]]. **⚠️ Confirmed duplicate**: `mhE_5qlJ0KU` ("Изменил двушку" #293) turned out to be a re-edited remake of already-processed `C4lUAfJyyb0` (#170) — same 69m² Odintsovo case, identical electrical-panel-niche formula and every worked number, explicitly framed in-video as an editing-style experiment by a different video editor. Logged `duplicate_skipped`, not re-extracted. **⚠️ Named-individual content excluded**: `pNhM-kKBy6A` contains a legal-dispute segment naming Karen Karapetyan (a recurring named-dispute target on this channel, already flagged repeatedly across this project's Category 5 processing) — the personal callout is not reproduced; only generalizable content extracted.

- **⚠️ Bathtub/shower sizing explicitly tied to occupant height**: 1700×700mm bathtub for an "average height" client; shower minimum 1500mm (ideal 1600-1700mm) length, minimum 900mm width — more explicit height-linked figures than this store's existing general sizing content. **Washing-machine-under-sink-countertop height-sensitivity caution**: the resulting elevated sink height isn't suitable for every client height — verify against the actual occupant before committing. [source: pNhM-kKBy6A]
- **⚠️ Ventilation shaft vs. duct box, the underlying safety mechanism spelled out**: never chase (штробить) into a true shared ventilation shaft, even a thin section — debris can fall down the shaft into units below, and the responsible party is fined. [source: u3UuZN9LHg0]
- **⚠️ Third documented instance of the "future-flexible planning" technique family**: pre-install reinforced wall backing (закладные) for a Swedish/gymnastic wall ladder *together with* hidden electrical for a TV zone at the same wall location, letting the client choose or later swap between the two without redoing the wall. [source: u3UuZN9LHg0]
- **⚠️ Cross-traffic-through-a-seating-zone elimination**: when an opening's position forces foot traffic directly in front of a TV-viewing seating area, relocate the *opening* (not just the furniture) to route traffic around the seating zone entirely. [source: u3UuZN9LHg0]
- **Deliberate flush-alignment exception, a second concrete instance**: leaving a step between two wall segments specifically because a piece of furniture (a closet) needs that step as its own back wall — reinforces the ~10%-exception rule already in this store. [source: u3UuZN9LHg0]
- **⚠️ Precise switch-reachability formula: 90cm from the floor, 15cm from the opening's edge** — demonstrated as the figures needed to find a switch reliably without looking. **Window-reveal outlet clearance: 10-15cm from the windowsill's own plane**, not flush against it. [source: eezwcNG-1qI]
- **⚠️ Bed-away-from-window reasoning made explicit**: cold air drafts off large glazing plus nearby radiators drying the air make a window-adjacent sleep zone genuinely uncomfortable — keep the bed set back, put a daylight-dependent zone (makeup vanity) near the window instead. [source: eezwcNG-1qI]
- **⚠️ Hygienic-shower/bidet-spray handedness, mechanism explained**: for a right-handed person, TP holder stays on the dominant (right) side, but the hygienic-shower spray goes on the *opposite* (left) side — the dominant hand washes while the non-dominant hand holds the spray wand. A more mechanism-explicit refinement of this store's existing handedness guidance. [source: eezwcNG-1qI]
- **⚠️ Multi-function combo-appliance cost caution**: an all-in-one unit can cost ~3× a single-function equivalent — buy separate appliances matched to actual usage instead of paying a premium for unused capability. **Plisse (pleated) shades specifically recommended for loggia glazing** — allows partial open/close combinations in one shade; this channel's standard loggia specification. [source: eezwcNG-1qI]
- **Real comparative pricing data point (2024, self-reported)**: a client paid a competitor 225,000 RUB for a design project (vs. this channel's own ~160,000 RUB estimate) plus ~25,000 RUB/m² construction (vs. this channel's own ~20,000 RUB/m², dated 2021) — record as a documented comparison, not a verified market benchmark. [source: eezwcNG-1qI]

### Remainder-Pool Round 2 — Fourth (Final) Batch (3 extracted + 1 no-captions skip, added 2026-08-20) — closes out Round 2's 16-video list

Full detail in each source's own extraction note: [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_4omxjEA7LaI_best_worst_solutions_rental_vs_own_283|4omxjEA7LaI]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_hVFmcw1H2Rk_bedroom_design_sequence_masterclass_314|hVFmcw1H2Rk]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_GtOQ7h1p5qc_pure_design_madness_70sqm_303|GtOQ7h1p5qc]]. `yt_HE4u2vprC88` ("Ремонт квартиры в белых тонах. Отзыв заказчика" #514) had no captions via either method — genuine no-captions failure, `skipped`.

- **⚠️⚠️ Standout finding — a complete, precisely sequenced 12-item bedroom design formula** (`hVFmcw1H2Rk`, #314): door → switch → wardrobe → false wall → curtain zone → bed → nightstands → bedside electrical → night light → TV zone → TV electrical → makeup table, each step locking in real dimensions the next depends on. Includes the most complete radiator-niche formula found in this store to date (15cm lower return, niche height = inter-axial + 20cm, 5cm minimum upper return) and a second independent corroboration of the 90cm/15cm switch-height formula. **Confirmed not a duplicate** of the title-similar `Rlyx2F7Aaxg` (#315) — entirely different format (from-scratch methodology vs. defect critique).
- **Paint-color reversibility, directional risk**: lighter→darker is cheap; darker→lighter is expensive — default to lighter when unsure. **Porcelain-to-laminate transition durability mismatch**: keep an entire high-wear/wet-adjacent zone in one durable material rather than mixing in laminate at the boundary. **AC "sitting under" clarifying distinction**: direct-airflow-path placement causes drafts; being physically beneath the unit but outside the airflow path gets comfortable mixed air — resolves a genuine common confusion. [source: 4omxjEA7LaI]
- **Flooring bevel/chamfer choice is material-dependent**: always beveled for laminate (hides seam gapping), case-by-case for quartz-vinyl/LVT (glued LVT rarely gaps; a bevel can look artificial on some textures). **"Functional decor" named concept**: an item that's both genuinely functional and a decorative accent — good design efficiency, but can feel visually tiring in long-term personal use despite photographing well for rental listings. **Closet depth tiers: 60cm basic, 70cm for bulky wardrobes, 80cm for sliding-door systems** (85cm+ flagged as wasted area). [source: 4omxjEA7LaI]
- **⚠️ Handle-free-appliance-system consistency caution**: paying a premium for handle-free cabinet fronts, then adding mismatched exposed handles to some appliances in the same run, undermines the whole investment — commit to the system across every appliance, not selectively. **⚠️ Black radiator-grille color caution**: makes accumulated dust *more* visible, not less — a lighter/neutral grille hides dust better. **⚠️ Door-swing-direction-consistency across a hallway's full door sequence** — people develop a directional expectation; a door swinging opposite its neighbors is a real usability friction even if independently justified. [source: GtOQ7h1p5qc]
- **⚠️ Door-open-limiter failure timeline, concrete**: a rubber wall-stopper absorbing 100% of impact through the door handle starts wobbling after 2-3 uses, typically needing replacement within 1-2 weeks — extends the existing door-stopper leverage-failure finding (`Rlyx2F7Aaxg`) with a real timeline. **⚠️ Shower-enclosure bottom-seal omission caution**: a premium enclosure lacking a basic bottom threshold/door-sweep seal (present even on budget balcony enclosures) lets water pool outside it. **⚠️ Backsplash material-matching mechanism**: porcelain tile must cover a sink-adjacent zone even in an otherwise-plastered open kitchen-living space — decorative plaster there absorbs water, cracks, and delaminates within about a year. [source: GtOQ7h1p5qc]

### Planning Rules — Floor-Plan Red Flags at Purchase Time (Kruglov/Ontario batch, added 2026-08-20)

> [!NOTE]
> Kruglov/Ontario batch, video 4 of 4 (`09aHjDgl-vk`, #315-range upload, 2026-05-01). This content is genuinely cross-cutting — an apartment-purchase-stage floor-plan checklist that touches many rooms/topics at once (layout, corridor, orientation, structure, plumbing routing, kitchen sizing) rather than belonging to any single room/systems wiki page — so it stays in this store's chronological Planning Rules record per the routing-default convention's own carve-out for cross-cutting content, consistent with the many prior "Planning Rules" batches already accumulated here (see Topic Index above). **Flagged, not resolved this session**: the "Planning Rules" sub-topic has been accumulating in this store well past the store's own 3+-source page-creation threshold (dozens of prior batches, unresolved since 2026-08-05) — out of scope for this 4-video batch to resolve by building a new top-level page, but worth surfacing again explicitly rather than silently adding one more batch to an already-overdue backlog. No pricing content in this source. `single-account`, `unverified` throughout. [source: [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_09aHjDgl-vk_kruglov_floor_plan_signs|YT_09aHjDgl-vk]]]

- **⚠️ Curved/rounded facade walls or glazing segments are a purchase-time red flag.** Furniture (sofas, cabinets, wardrobes) generally doesn't fit a curved bay; baseboards and curtain rods aren't manufactured for a curved run; an internal curved decorative wall added on top of an already-curved facade compounds the planning difficulty rather than adding to it linearly. **Cost mechanism**: curved-wall finishing is billed per linear meter rather than per square meter, since plastering a curved wall takes roughly 2-3× the labor of a straight one (no trowel exists pre-matched to an arbitrary radius); custom furniture built to fit a curved bay commonly runs 3-10× the cost of an equivalent standard piece.
- **⚠️ Narrow "spread-wing" (распашонка) room layouts: avoid a room width under ~2.5m even if the listed square footage looks adequate.** Worked example: a 2m mattress plus headboard consumes ~2.2-2.3m, leaving only 20-30cm of walking clearance beside the bed in a 2.5m-wide room — genuinely too tight for comfortable daily use, and if a wall-mounted TV is added, reaching the bed's far side means walking across the bed itself. A wider spread-wing variant (the source's example: a П-44 three-room layout with ~3.5m room width) doesn't have this problem — the issue is the specific narrow width, not the spread-wing layout family generally.
- **⚠️ Always request a developer's actual dimensioned floor plan (width and length per room, not just total m²) before buying.** A furnished floor-plan rendering can mislead by drawing furniture at an incorrect scale (e.g. a bed rendered to look like it fits a 2m mattress, but actually drawn at a 1.5m scale) with no real recourse afterward — the furnished rendering typically isn't part of the binding purchase contract (ДДУ in Russia), so a buyer has nothing enforceable to point to once the mismatch is discovered post-purchase. Independently verify real dimensions rather than trusting a furnished rendering's proportions.
- **⚠️ Long, narrow corridors are a layout downside worth actively avoiding at purchase time.** Some layout families (the source's example: П-44 variants) allow merging the living room with the corridor — usually straightforward to get approved — eliminating the narrow corridor and enlarging the living room, at the cost of the living room becoming a pass-through space (isolated bedrooms stay isolated either way). Prefer a layout that avoids the long-narrow-corridor problem from the start over relying on this fix being available later.
- **⚠️ Natural insolation (solar orientation) as a Moscow-specific room/apartment-selection factor**, mechanism explained (direct sunlight contributes to vitamin D, serotonin, and melatonin production): **South** = maximum sun/heat — a real winter heating assist, but summer overheating that forces either running AC continuously or keeping blinds closed most of the day. **Southwest/West** = still bright and warm, milder than full south, but panoramic glazing on this exposure can get hot enough in summer to burn skin on contact. **North** = even, soft, no direct-sun light — cooler summers with less AC need, but noticeably darker and colder in winter. **Northeast/East** = comfortable morning sun with softer light the rest of the day, avoiding harsh afternoon/evening glare. Framed as a factor to weigh (not a hard pass/fail) both when comparing whole-apartment options and when deciding which room (office vs. living room vs. bedroom) to place on which exposure.
- **Load-bearing walls, columns, and non-widenable openings materially restrict furniture/layout planning and reduce resale liquidity** — buyers recognize and discount a heavily structurally-constrained layout, so check for these before buying, not just before renovating.
- **⚠️ Verify sewer riser-stack placement before buying, not just after.** Documented case: a sewer riser positioned directly beside a kitchen window (confirmed via the developer's own plans, not a later AC-condensate line as initially assumed) — an objectively bad placement forcing the buyer to choose between installing an inconvenient "sololift"-type macerator pump or rerouting a long new sewer run to relocate the kitchen sink elsewhere. Prefer a layout with sensibly-placed risers when a comparable alternative exists.
- **⚠️ A small "kitchen-niche" alcove labeled with a specific square footage (e.g. "5 m²" or "7 m²") on a floor plan often can't actually fit a full kitchen setup plus a table, despite how it reads on paper.** Verify with actual planning software before assuming it will work (the source names a specific paid app it uses for this, framed as a non-endorsement) — if it genuinely doesn't fit, the practical options are relocating the dining table to the living room, or combining the kitchen niche with the living room outright rather than fighting the space as a separate enclosed room.
